# Apps ledger: connectors, apps in a tab, ParaView

Rows follow [mcp-apps-surfaces-plan.md](mcp-apps-surfaces-plan.md): decisions `CN` (connectors), `MA` (app host), `PV` (ParaView) and `CM` (looking at views and comments, the plan's addendum), scenarios `AQ`. Cross-stream wire shapes are in [mcp-apps-contracts.md](mcp-apps-contracts.md).

- **Branch:** `claude/parallel-agents-plan-uzpzm0` in clio-agent, gact-tui, clio-kit, clio-agent-marketplace and clio-schemas.
- **Bases:**

  | Repository | Base |
  | --- | --- |
  | clio-agent | `develop` at `54ae350af` |
  | gact-tui | `develop` at `d13393f13` |
  | clio-kit | `c22c7e4` |
  | clio-agent-marketplace | `bc5ddb2` (the clio-agent submodule pin) |
  | clio-schemas | `develop` at `fd04806` |

- **References** (read-only clones): ext-apps at `ea3e9d0`, Burette at `b1ac8ae`, ltspice-mcp at `9455f38`.

Status vocabulary (as in the [acceptance ledger](acceptance-ledger.md)):

- `passed (live)`: exercised against a running CLIO, with the sessions, commands and screenshots that were read.
- `tests only`: covered by the named tests and not exercised live.
- `static`: checked by reading the tree.
- `done`: a recorded decision with no behaviour of its own.
- `superseded`: replaced by the row it points to; kept so the history is honest.
- `open`, or `blocked` with the reason.

Live scenarios run on Sonnet 5.5 through the `claude_code` provider. **The plain-request rule:** a prompt that names the tool, its arguments or the steps verifies the mechanism only, and is recorded as "mechanism verified with a tool-directed prompt; not evidence of agent judgement". Only a plain request counts as evidence of the agent's choices. Every Qualification row says which kind its prompts were.

**How this ledger was made.** The work ran as parallel streams, each on `ws/<stream>` with its own stack, then integration qualification streams on the merged code, then fix streams. Each kept its rows in a fragment; this file merges them and the fragments are deleted. Row IDs keep the stream's own prefix so they match commit messages and step files. Where a later row changed an earlier one, the earlier row is marked `superseded` with a pointer, and the current behaviour is the later row.

## Session environment

| ID | Fact | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ENV1 | Docker 29.9.2 runs in the session (overlayfs, cgroup v1). Docker Hub answers anonymous pulls with 429, so images come from `public.ecr.aws/docker/library/*` | `docker run --rm public.ecr.aws/docker/library/debian:bookworm-slim` | done |
| ENV2 | Headless ParaView 6 for the session's machine is `/opt/pv6` (paraview 6.2.1 `py312hbd06571_0`, Python 3.12.16, vtk-base 9.7.2), installed with `micromamba create -y -p /opt/pv6 -c conda-forge paraview>=6 python=3.12` (`MAMBA_ROOT_PREFIX=/opt/mamba`). It is the Qt/EGL build and renders offscreen through the system `libosmesa6` (Ubuntu 24.04, `libOSMesa.so.8`) with `VTK_DEFAULT_OPENGL_WINDOW=vtkOSOpenGLRenderWindow`. Inside the cluster's `node08` the same package comes from conda-forge at build time, pinned `paraview=6.2.1 python=3.12 pip`, with Debian's `libosmesa6` | `/opt/pv6/conda-meta/history`; the `node08` Dockerfile stage; self-check PVC-S4 (pv-cluster ENV2) | done |
| ENV3 | Container egress: containers on a Docker bridge reach the internet directly and need only the session CA bundle (`$SSL_CERT_FILE`); the host's agent proxy on 127.0.0.1:34197 is not reachable from a bridge and is not needed. `cluster.sh up` mounts the CA read-only and the entrypoint trusts it; the build passes it as a BuildKit secret, never into a layer. `CLIO_PV_CLUSTER_HTTPS_PROXY` covers a network that needs a proxy | curl to pypi.org with and without `--cacert` (200 / error 60); `/dev/tcp/172.17.0.1/34197` refused (pv-cluster ENV3) | done |
| ENV4 | Docker DNS in the session is occasionally flaky (sshd on `login` failed to resolve the node alias about 1 jump in 12). The cluster network therefore has fixed addresses (`10.208.8.0/24`) written into each node's `/etc/hosts`, and `up` waits until `login` resolves the package index | 12 of 12 `ssh -J ares ares-compute-08 hostname` after the fix, 1 of 8 failed before; `cluster.sh check` 20/20 (pv-cluster ENV4) | done |
| ENV5 | ngspice 42 (KLU) is installed for ltspice-mcp; the openssh client and server are installed | `ngspice --version`; GR8 (`ngspice -b -r` on the fixture netlist) | done |
| ENV6 | The check browser has WebGL 1 and 2: Playwright's Chromium 153 (`/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`) draws through ANGLE on SwiftShader with no flag. `scripts/cloud/ui_check.mjs` passes `--enable-unsafe-swiftshader` anyway, because Chrome is deprecating the automatic software fallback; the renderer is the same. The ParaView stand-in host needed `--use-angle=swiftshader --enable-unsafe-swiftshader` for trame's local view (PV-F2) | `ui_check.mjs --steps …/webgl.mjs --label grounds-webgl` printed `WebGL 2.0 … SwiftShader Device (Subzero)` (grounds ENV3) | done |
| ENV7 | The 17800 noise is gone: `ui_check.mjs` seeds the UI's remembered service (`localStorage["clio.recent-connections"]`) with `--agent` before the first load, so the UI never tries the product default. Product code and defaults are untouched | before: 11 `127.0.0.1:17800` failures (`grounds-base`); after: `consoleErrors: []`, `failedRequests: []` (`grounds-seed`, `grounds-webgl`) (grounds ENV4) | done |
| ENV8 | A Docker network created or removed anywhere on the machine makes Chromium abort every request in flight with `net::ERR_NETWORK_CHANGED`. `ui_check.mjs` prints `networkChanged: true` and says to rerun, so such a run is repeated, not debugged | `docker events`; the `aq32-spice` run of 08:42 (grounds ENV5) | done |
| ENV9 | The machine is shared: 4 CPUs and 15 GB for all the streams (ten at once in the first phase). Load average ran 14 to 68 during runs, with 1 to 2 GB free at times: the service stream's stack had its Vite server OOM-killed and Chromium's renderer crash on four runs (service AQ30), and gact-tui `pnpm typecheck` needs `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=4096`. Disk ran at 94 to 98 % (1 to 2.7 GB free); each stack's clio-core preallocates about 1 GB, so stacks ran with `CLIO_ARC_CTE_FILE_CAPACITY=48MB` or `128MB` and removed their store after stopping. Runs that failed on a timeout or a crashed renderer under load were rerun unchanged and say so | grounds ENV6, service AQ30, q-apps and q-conn stack notes, QG-Q5 | done |
| ENV10 | Isolated stacks and live slots: each stream exported `CLIO_CLOUD_ISOLATE=1 AGENT_PORT=88NN UI_PORT=52NN FIXTURE_PORT…` and ran `scripts/cloud/stack.sh`, so its config, sessions and saved connections live in its own `.cloud-run/xdg/`. With isolation, transcripts are in `.cloud-run/xdg/state/clio-agent/server/messages/sess_*.json`, not under `xdg/config` (grounds ENV7). `/home/user/wt/live-slot.sh acquire <stream>` and `release <stream>` limits the machine to three running stacks; `release` also frees the slot's clio-core store | `live-slot.sh`; every stream's resources note | done |
| ENV11 | The session's container restarted once during the work. The reopen stream's first hang (10:05, `sess_8d7619bc9f0e`) happened before it; its passing runs on that session (09:55 to 10:09) are superseded by runs on freshly started stacks afterwards | reopen Open note; AQ25, AQ26 | done |
| ENV12 | The session scratchpad is shared by every stream. pv-cluster's cleanup (`rm -rf <scratchpad>/*`, about 09:13) also deleted another stream's test home (`scratchpad/home1/.clio/paraview/3.0.0/`, a running node half's folder); that node half was left alone for its owner. Later streams kept scratch data inside their own worktrees | pv-cluster Resources | done |

## Facts

The facts the plan's "What the ledger records" asks for, each with its source row, then the other facts the work relied on.

| CNF1 | **The credential store** for a connection's credentials is `<user config>/mcp_oauth_tokens.json` (owner-only, `0600` from creation), written by `tools/mcp_oauth_storage.py::DurableFileTokenStorage`, keyed by server URL and saved-connection name. CN8 secrets go there in their own entry `["clio-connector-secrets", <connector id>]` (`connectors/secrets.py`), so they never collide with a sign-in. Headers typed into "Add an MCP connection" live in `mcp.yaml` (also `0600`); a connector's secret never does: `mcp.yaml` holds only `${need.<key>}` | conn-be; `tests/test_connectors/test_materialize_and_secrets.py::test_a_secret_stays_a_placeholder_until_the_server_starts` | static |
| F7, CNF2 | **Every place "MCP sign-in required; connect this service in CLIO" was raised or shown** at the base. Raised (one message, `MCPTransportError`): `tools/mcp_runtime.py:100` `_oauth_provider_from_config`, eagerly while building the client when the auth block has `require_existing_tokens` and the store has no tokens (the namespace then failed to mount); and `tools/mcp_durable_oauth.py:70` `DurableOAuthProvider.async_auth_flow`, the same condition at request time. `require_existing_tokens: True` comes from `gact/mcp_auth.py:71` `managed_oauth()`. Shown: no dedicated copy; it reached the person as the failed call's error, as HTTP 422 `mcp_spec_invalid` from `routes/mcp.py:475` and `:837`, as a reconnect refusal at `:653`, and as a `<sid>_error` catalog row from `routes/catalog.py:116`. One test matched it (`tests/test_gact/test_mcp_sign_in.py:102`). gact-tui had no copy. Replaced by the typed `MCPSignInNeeded` (CB11); AQ51 | grounds F7, conn-be CNF2; `grep -rn` at the base | static |
| F8, CNF3 | **How the resolver takes a person's additions.** `plugins/resolve.py` `resolve_agent(catalog, spec, marketplace, delta)` applies a `Delta(add, remove)` after the agent's own plugins (`_resolve_set`; each add is `source="user"`, then removals and the requirement cascade). The delta is composed in `plugins/service.py` `resolve(ref, delta)`: a user agent's `UserAgent.add/remove` chain (`plugins/store.py:97`, `<plugins root>/user-agents/<id>.json`) flattened by `_user_delta`, then `.then(session delta)` from session metadata `agent_delta` (`gact/agents/resolution.py`). | F1 | **Draft spec, open-link** (`ea3e9d0`): view → host `ui/open-link {url}`, result `{}` (`McpUiOpenLinkResult {isError?}`), errors JSON-RPC `-32000`; host capability `hostCapabilities.openLinks: {}`; SDK `app.openLink`, `OPEN_LINK_METHOD`. | F2 | **Draft spec, download:** `ui/download-file {contents: (EmbeddedResource \| ResourceLink)[]}`; an `EmbeddedResource` is `{type: "resource", resource: {uri, mimeType, text \| blob}}` (filename from the last segment of `resource.uri`), a `ResourceLink` is `{type: "resource_link", uri, name, mimeType}` (the host fetches it). Result `{}` (`McpUiDownloadFileResult {isError?, …}`), errors `-32000`. Host capability `downloadFile: {}`. The host SHOULD confirm and sanitise the filename and MAY refuse by size or policy. SDK `app.downloadFile`. Draft only | grounds F2, host MA16; `apps.mdx` 1118–1183, `spec.types.ts` 182–200 | static |

There is no host capability for app tools (independent of the host's `serverTools`). The spec's limits are recommendations (50 tools, 30 s, 10 MB); this plan uses 64 tools, 64 KiB per schema and no timeout. | F9, MA23-F1 | **How the app routes' capability is issued and checked.** Issued in `gact/mcp_apps.py` `MCPAppRegistry.register`: `app_instance_id = secrets.token_urlsafe(18)`, `data_ref = secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`, both only on the transcript's `mcp_app` part. Checked by `resolve` in `register_mcp_app_routes` (`gact/mcp_app_routes.py`): the session must exist, then `MCPAppRegistry.get(sid, app_id, data_ref)` requires the record to be live (not closed or expired), owned by `sid`, and `secrets.compare_digest(record.data_ref, data_ref)`; any miss is the same non-oracular 404 `not_found`. `get` refreshes idle time; `claim_close` makes the same three-way check and removes atomically. The service routes call the same `resolve` (MA23-D1 carries `data_ref` as a path segment). At the base the sandbox page was served on another loopback name and only to a loopback or Tauri referrer; `BearerAuthMiddleware` checked only `http` scopes and admitted loopback peers without a token; `OriginGuardMiddleware` checked `http` and `websocket`. The sandbox host is now per server (MA-sandbox) | grounds F9, service MA23-F1; `gact/mcp_apps.py` 240–330, `gact/mcp_app_routes.py` 48–100, `gact/auth.py`, `gact/origin_guard.py` at `54ae350af` | static |
| PV-F1 | **ParaView, trame and trame-vtk qualified:** ParaView 6.2.1 (`/opt/pv6`, ENV2; Python 3.12.16; VTK 9.7.2; ships wslink 2.6.0 and aiohttp 3.14.5), node half started with `pvpython --dr --force-offscreen-rendering`; trame 4.0.0 (trame-client 4.1.4, trame-server 4.1.1) and trame-vtk 2.11.17. On the cluster: ParaView 6.2.1 at `/opt/apps/paraview/6.2.1` behind `module load paraview` | pv-server; `pvpython --version`, `env.json` | done |
| PV-F2 | **The software-rendering flag.** trame's local (WebGL) view needed `--use-angle=swiftshader --enable-unsafe-swiftshader` in the stand-in host's headless Chromium (`getContext('webgl2')` then succeeds). The integrated `ui_check.mjs` launches with `--enable-unsafe-swiftshader` (ENV6); pv-server's own `UI_CHECK_CHROMIUM_ARGS` option was dropped for it at the merge | pv-server; stand-in `report.webgl: true` | done |
| PVC1, PV-F3 | **Cluster fixture form: containers** (Docker 29.9.2), not the loopback-sshd fallback. Three images from one multi-stage `Dockerfile` on `public.ecr.aws/docker/library/debian:bookworm-slim`: `clio-pv-cluster:login` 259 MB, `:locked` 168 MB, `:node08` 3.23 GB (2.4 GB is ParaView's conda environment). `login` and `node08` share one home volume; `node08` is reachable only through `login` and has no package index; `locked` is password-only. What containers cannot model is left to the local phase: a scheduler, two-factor sign-in, a GPU node, network distance, a shared parallel filesystem | pv-cluster PVC1, pv-server PV-F3; `cluster.sh build`, `status` | done |

| ID | Fact | Source | Status |

| F5 | The base host advertised `hostCapabilities: {serverTools, serverResources, logging, sandbox}` and `displayMode: "inline"`, `availableDisplayModes: ["inline"]`, not `updateModelContext`, `message`, `openLinks` or `downloadFile`. Burette reports its selection only when `updateModelContext` is advertised, so AQ33 needed MA17 (MA11-D1) | grounds; gact-tui `mcp-app-surface.tsx` 398–414, Burette `burette-hosted-app.ts` 54–56 | static |
| F11 | A saved MCP connection that is not "Available in every conversation" (`always_load: false`, the API default) is `available`, its tools are not in a new conversation's catalog, and a stdio server starts only when a conversation that uses it opens. The first AQ32 attempt (`sess_20476c8a1354`) never saw the server. Connectors replace this with enable and scope (CN10) | grounds | static |
| F12 | The base sandbox's inner frame was `sandbox="allow-scripts allow-forms"` with `allow-same-origin` filtered out, though the draft spec says the view frame MUST have `allow-scripts allow-same-origin` with host and view on different origins (`apps.mdx` 488–497; `basic-host` `sandbox.ts:46`). Burette reads `localStorage` and died on it (AQ33 early). Granting the flag was safe only on an origin that is neither the UI nor the API; resolved by MA-sandbox | grounds | static |
| RO-F3 | A view's `tools/call` and a replay use the same path: `_run_bound` runs `SyncMCPToolExecutor.call_tool_result` in the session's tool context, which applies the permission gate, the tool observer and the MCP App observer as for an agent's call (`tools/execution.py::_call_tool_inner`); outside a turn the gate's ask is the ordinary permission card | reopen | static |
| MA8-D2 | "Live" for the context block is the registry's admitted records for the session (`records_for_session`): the registry pins tab and surface records and the UI closes a superseded inline app, so the admitted records are the live set | ctx-tools | done |
| PV5-F1, PVC8 | **A ParaView 6.2.1 pvserver** keeps its pipeline only while a client is connected and exits when its first client disconnects, even with `--multi-clients` ("Client connected. … Exiting…"). The fixture's pvserver form therefore runs `pvserver --multi-clients --server-port=11111 --opengl-window-backend OSMesa` with `hold_dataset.py` holding `pulse_series.pvd` and a contour `pressure isosurface`; the node half joins as one more client and must not disconnect the holder. The server itself never starts a pvserver or uses `--multi-clients` (PV2); the plan is amended | pv-cluster PVC8, pv-server PV5-F1; host test with three clients; self-check PVC-S10 | done |
| PVC4 | `login`'s Python (3.11) deliberately differs from `node08`'s pvpython (3.12), as on real sites, so building `node08`'s environment from `login` fetches wheels for the target interpreter (`--python-version 3.12 --platform manylinux2014_x86_64 --only-binary=:all:`). `pvpython` and `pvserver` 6.2.1 accept `--venv PATH` | pv-cluster; self-check PVC-S6 | done |
| PVC5 | The `prepare` line is `module load paraview` (Lmod 8.6.19). `module` is a bash function defined in every bash shell, including non-interactive `ssh host 'cmd'` and `bash -lc`, but not in `sh -c`, as on real clusters, so the front runs the prepare line in bash | pv-cluster; `ssh node08 "sh -c 'module load paraview'"` → `module: not found` | done |

| A2-F5 | Before `ctx-tools` merged, the agent building a surface guessed the channel key `time` (a view's keys are unknown before it runs). With MA8 the whole report (`content` and `structuredContent`) reaches the next turn, so an open view's keys are visible; a surface for a view not yet opened still names keys from the server's description (CT-D3) | a2ui A2-P1 | done |
| FX-F1 | **Root cause of the external-call hang** (reopen's and a2ui's open rows, q-apps Q-F3). A sync MCP executor chose its loop when built: the bound app's, or else a private loop. Task recovery after a restart (`ExternalTasks._recovery_client`) and the commands listing (`mcp_prompt_commands.session_executor`) built workspace fleets with no app bound, which connected the namespace's proxy session on a private loop; a product call on the application loop then borrowed that kept-alive stdio session, writing to a session whose reader ran on another loop (idle: the server never got the call; or stopped when a superseded fleet closed). The caller waited forever in `_run_foreground_coroutine`, which by design has no deadline on the application loop (#1225) and, outside a turn, no cancellation. Fixed by FX-D1 | fix; `tests/test_tools/test_executor_loop.py::test_a_call_on_the_server_loop_reaches_a_server_the_executor_connected` times out at the base and passes with the fix | done |
| FX-F2 | On one loop, a closed connection fails its pending call at once (`Connection closed`); `ExternalTasks._call` records `task_state_uncertain`, which the replay had reported as `view_not_opened`, a wrong reason (FX-D3) | fix | done |
| FX-F6 | q-apps' live hangs (`sess_e854ab93519f`, `sess_bfe650979941`: two parallel `boards_open_board` after a restart sat 10+ minutes, `clio-tool_0/1` in `Future.result` under `_run_foreground_coroutine`, both `clio-sync-mcp-tool-executor` loops idle) match FX-F1. Before the fix the agent had 2 private executor threads after a re-save and a conversation open; after it, 0 after a restart, a reopen, a re-save and a turn | fix; `py-spy dump` before and after | done |
| FX-F7 | The first `GET /v1/sessions/{sid}/work?cursor=0` after a restart answered 500: the async route rehydrated the ledger on the loop thread and `task_history.pending_mail` refused it (`LoopThreadStoreWrite 'task.mail.read'`). Fixed by FX-D7 | fix; `agent.log` after the 16:05 restart | done |
| FX-F3 | `tests/test_docs/test_env_reference.py` already failed at the base (five failures: four stale generated references and `tools.script_media_max` with no note); conn-be regenerated the references and FX-D4 added the note | fix; base run `5 failed, 21 passed`, after `26 passed` | done |
| QC-F | Through the in-process FastMCP proxy, `on_discover` turns an upstream's `MCPSignInNeeded` into a protocol error, so a session's listing of a connector that needs a sign-in lost the type (a direct listing keeps it). Fixed by QC1 | q-conn AQ6 first run, `sess_52f94ec456e5` | done |

## Decisions

### Connectors (CN1 to CN12)

| ID | Decision | Source | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CN1 | A connector is one entry: who it is, where its server is, what it needs, the plugin it brings. Entries are `catalogs/connectors/*.yaml` in clio-agent with a JSON Schema; `GET /v1/connectors` returns them with each one's state. Three ship: `burette`, `spice`, `paraview` (GR1). Backend in CB1 to CB4, CB13; UI in CN12 | plan CN1; plan default "Where connector entries live" | CB1, GR1; AQ1, AQ2 | passed (live) |
| CN2 | Four kinds of need (`address`, `sign-in`, `secret`, `value`), required or optional, with default and `agent_settable`; placement rules in CB1 | plan CN2 | CB1, CB3; AQ3, AQ5 | passed (live) |
| CN3 | One state per connector per person, in words wherever it appears. Server: CB8. UI: one function, `connectorStatus` (`web/src/lib/connector-presentation.ts`), gives "Ready, N tools", "Needs server address and a sign-in", "Can’t be reached" (reason beside it in the card) and "Not enabled", in the Extensions row, the setup card, the conversation card and the conversation's inline line; `StatusIndicator` always has a label. A service a connector starts shows the connector's logo in the conversation's service list (`McpServiceIcon`) | plan CN3; conn-ui, conn-be | `connector-setup.test.tsx` "connector state in words"; AQ1 to AQ4, AQ6 | passed (live) |
| CN4 | The setup card is the Extensions detail pane (`connector-pane.tsx`): logo, name, state, description, source, tool count, the plugin it brings, then one row per need (`connector-need-rows.tsx`). Address or value: a text field, or a picker with `options`. Secret: a masked field that is always empty; once saved, "Saved. Type a new one to replace it". Sign-in: a button opening the existing `McpSignInDialog` for the connection named by the connector id, with "Signed in" or "Not signed in". Save sends only what changed (`lib/connector-draft.ts`); an empty secret keeps the stored one, an emptied address clears it | plan CN4, CN8; conn-ui | `connector-setup.test.tsx` "the setup card in Extensions"; AQ3, AQ4, AQ5, AQ11 | passed (live) |
| CN5 | The same card in the conversation: the call waits, the card appears, the same call continues, a decline is a typed refusal. Server: CB10, CB11, CBO2, QC1. UI: a pending interaction of kind `connector_setup` rendered in the attention dock by `ConnectorSetupResponse` (an `AskCard` with the missing rows, Continue and Decline); the inline line `ConnectorSetupActivity` says "Set up X", "waiting for you" with a jump to the card, then "set up", "declined" or "cancelled". After a sign-in through the card, the card submits by itself when no other row is missing | plan CN5; plan default "A declined card"; conn-ui, conn-be | `connector-setup.test.tsx` "the setup card in the conversation"; AQ6, AQ7, AQ12 | passed (live) |
| CN6 | `set_connector_value` (CB12, H6). UI: each field shows the person's draft or else the service's value, so an agent's value appears when the list is read again; every `connector.*` event on the session stream refreshes connectors, servers, tools, plugins, agents and resolution (`queryInvalidationKeysForEvent`), `connector.setup.*` refreshes pending interactions; an `agent_settable` need says "The agent can set this" | plan CN6; plan default "Who may call set_connector_value" | `connector-setup.test.tsx` "shows a value the agent set"; AQ8 | passed (live) |
| CN7 | Applying a change: CB9 (rebind in place), H6 (one apply per call, one listing for every fleet) | plan CN7 | AQ8, H6 | passed (live) |
| CN8 | Secrets: stored in the credential store (CNF1), never returned to the UI, in no model text, transcript, question record or log (CB14) | plan CN8 | AQ5 | passed (live) |
| CN9 | A plugin that follows a connector: `follows` in `ai.iowarp.clio` (CB5), additions computed from enabled connectors (CNF3, CB4), a running conversation follows (CB6). UI: a plugin with `follows` is left out of the Extensions plugin rows, the agent's "Add package" and "Add plugin" pickers (`offeredPlugins` in `agent-composition-view.tsx`) and the compose-a-plugin picker (`extension-add-dialog.tsx`); the connector's card names it as "Brings plugin"; a carrier is labelled "From connector <name>" (CBO3) *Amended (CS-D2, CS-D4):* enabling adds the plugin to no agent; it is listed (with a carrier for every enabled connector) and offered under Connectors in a conversation's "Add plugin" picker and the agent editor while its connector is on, by the connector's name and logo; still left out of the Extensions plugin rows and Compose a plugin; resolution rows read "Connector you added" instead of "From connector" | plan CN9; conn-ui, conn-be | `connector-setup.test.tsx`; AQ10 | passed (live) |
| CN10 | Where it applies: the card's "Available in every conversation" switch; off, a checklist of installed agents. Enable sends `{scope: "all"}` or `{scope: {agents: [refs]}}` (CD2), Apply changes the scope, Turn off disables (CB7). The agents checklist was read, not driven live *Amended (CS-D1):* no scope. Enable has no body and only installs and readies; the card's "Where it applies" switch and checklist are replaced by "In conversations" (Enable / Turn off and how to add it to a conversation) | plan CN10; conn-ui | AQ2, AQ10 | passed (live) |
| CN11 | Logos: served with a script-free CSP (CB15). UI: `ConnectorLogo` reads the SVG through the repository (`GET /v1/connectors/{id}/logo`, carrying the service's credentials on every transport) and shows it as a `data:` URL in an `<img>`, where it cannot run script; no logo, or a logo that fails to load, shows `Monogram` (`primitives/monogram.tsx`, extracted from the profile badge in `navigation-footer.tsx`). Sources and terms: GR5, GR6 | plan CN11; conn-ui | `connector-setup.test.tsx` (monogram); AQ1 (logos, monograms "FS", "SS"), AQ11 ("SU") | passed (live) |
| CN12 | Extensions lists connectors first (`extensionEntries`), above plugins, marketplaces and skills. The "MCP connections" tab is now "Connectors"; a saved MCP connection with no connector of the same id still appears there, after the connectors. Heading "Connectors, plugins, marketplaces and skills for your agents." Add's menu has one "Add by URL or command" item opening the raw form, retitled the same; it creates a connector (`POST /v1/connectors`, scope `all`) when it holds only a Streamable HTTP address with automatic sign-in and no headers, or only a command with arguments and no environment; any advanced setting keeps it a raw MCP connection, because an entry cannot carry those (CD7) | plan CN12; conn-ui | `settings-tools.test.tsx`; AQ1, AQ11 | passed (live) |
| CB1 | **Entries.** Each file a list, validated by `catalogs/connectors/connector.schema.json` (JSON Schema 2020-12) and by placement rules the schema cannot say (`connectors/entries.py`): every `${need.<key>}` names a need, a sign-in is never substituted, a secret only in `headers`/`env`, a sign-in only for a URL server and at most one, no default or `agent_settable` for a secret or sign-in, `options` only on a value, the logo an SVG beside the file. An invalid entry is left out with a typed diagnostic (`connector_entry_invalid`, `connector_entry_duplicate`, `connector_file_unreadable`) returned by `GET /v1/connectors`; the others load. The catalog ships in the wheel (force-include to `clio_agent/_catalogs/connectors`) and the sdist | conn-be; CN1, CN2, CN11 | `tests/test_connectors/test_entries.py` | tests only |
| CB2 | **Fixtures outside the shipped catalog.** More catalog folders come from `connectors.catalog_dirs` (`CLIO_CONNECTOR_CATALOG_DIRS`, `PATH`-separated); a later folder cannot replace an earlier entry. The fixtures are `tests/fixtures/connectors/fixtures.yaml`: `fixture-plain` (no needs), `fixture-signin` (an `agent_settable` address and a sign-in against `tests/fixtures/oauth_mcp_server.py`), `fixture-secret` (a secret and an optional `agent_settable` address, followed by `fixture-secret-skills` in `tests/fixtures/connectors/marketplace`). Their command server is `tests/fixtures/connector_fixture_server.py`; `connector-fixtures.env.sh` sets the variables before `stack.sh start` | conn-be; plan fixtures | `docs/ENVIRONMENT.md`; `tests/test_connectors/conftest.py` | done |
| CB3 | **An enabled connector is a saved connection.** Its id is the connection's name and tool namespace; the `mcp.yaml` declaration carries `connector: <id>`, `connection_only: true`, the address and value needs written in, secrets as `${need.<key>}`, and CLIO's managed OAuth block for a sign-in need. An id already used by a connection the person saved is refused (`connection_name_taken`, 409) *Amended (CS-D1):* the saved connection is still what enabling writes; it reaches a conversation only through the plugin the person adds | conn-be; CN1, CN2, CN8 | `connectors/materialize.py`, `gact/connectors_service.py::save_connection` | tests only |
| CB4 | **One rule for scope.** A connector reaches agents only through a plugin that follows it, never by mounting on its own: an installed plugin with `follows: <id>`, else a content-free carrier CLIO builds (`connector-<id>`, marketplace `clio-connectors`). `scope: "all"` adds the carrier for every agent, `{agents: [refs]}` for those agents *Amended (CS-D2):* there is no scope rule. The resolver's catalog holds a carrier for every enabled connector; nothing adds it; a conversation's delta or an agent definition does | conn-be; CN9, CN10 | `tests/test_connectors/test_follow.py`; AQ2 (`connector-fixture-plain` pinned) | passed (live) |
| CB5 | **`follows`.** A new `ai.iowarp.clio` field; a plugin that follows a connector must bind it with `connections: {<id>: <id>}` (a manifest error otherwise). `PluginRecord.follows`, `GET /v1/plugins` rows and each plugin of a resolution carry it *Amended (CS-D3):* plugin rows also carry `connector: {id, name, logo_url, enabled, status, needs, reason, tool_count}`, resolution rows `connector: {id, name, logo_url}` | conn-be; CN9 | `tests/test_connectors/test_manifest_follows.py` | tests only |
| CB6 | **A running conversation follows.** The pin records the connectors it was resolved with (`agent_connectors`); a differing read pins again and holds the prompt basis (`plugin-change-delivery.md`'s `note` mode), so the change reaches the agent as the existing `plugin_change` injection and notice, and added tools run at once. A connector change sweeps the conversations open now (`gact/connector_follow.py`); any other follows when next read *Amended (CS-D3):* no `agent_connectors` stamp. A pin is followed when a plugin of a connector now off is in it, or a plugin dropped for `connector_off` can come back (`connectors.follow.drifted`); the sweep re-pins only such open conversations | conn-be; CN10 | AQ10 | passed (live) |
| CB7 | **Disabling keeps the saved connection** (still `connection_only`), so a conversation whose cached prompt lists the plugin until its next compaction does not fail with `mcp_connection_unavailable`; values and secrets are kept *Amended (CS-D3):* a conversation that has the plugin drops it from its resolution with the `removed` reason `connector_off` (its delta keeps it, so enabling brings it back) | conn-be; CN10 | `connectors_service.disable` | done |
| CB8 | **State in words.** `needs` when a required value or secret is empty or a required sign-in has no account (an optional empty need never holds it, plan default "Ready"); `unreachable` with a sentence when CLIO started or called the server and it did not answer (it wins over a missing sign-in, AQ4); otherwise `ready`. A disabled connector is never started. The server is checked once per configuration and again on `?refresh=true`; `tool_count` is set only when ready | conn-be; CN3, CN4 | `test_service.py::test_rows_say_what_is_missing_and_why_unreachable`; AQ3, AQ4 | passed (live) |
| CB9 | **Applying a change.** Every change rewrites the saved connection, rebuilds configuration-dependent fleets for new turns, and rebinds the namespace in place in every resident fleet (`gact/connector_rebind.py`): new spec and proxy, connection closed so a URL server reconnects and a command server restarts with its new arguments and environment, tools listed again. A fleet held by a running turn is kept until the turn ends (#1244), which is where a waiting call lives. Then the server is checked, open conversations follow, and `connector.changed` is published *Amended (CS-D3):* "open conversations follow" now means only those that have the connector's plugin | conn-be; CN7 | AQ8 (`connector_rebound namespace=fixture-secret`) | passed (live) |
| CB10 | **The setup card (server).** The tool-runtime permission gate is wrapped (`gact/connector_setup.py::with_connector_setup`, installed in `gact/runtime/app_state.py::resolve_tool_runtime`): after a connector's tool call is allowed, a connector that is not ready makes it wait on the tool thread as a permission waits (an in-memory row, a `threading.Event`, the turn's cancel token, no timeout). The durable Question task was not chosen: its record would hold what the person typed, which CN8 forbids. The row is a pending interaction of kind `connector_setup`, answered at `/interactions/connector:<id>/respond` (`submit` with `values`, or `decline`). Submit applies the values as Extensions does (CB9), also on a values-free submit after a sign-in (the fix for conn-be's first AQ6 failure); a submit leaving a required row empty answers 422. Decline returns `connector_declined: … (<id>) …`. Stopping the turn records the call as cancelled | conn-be; CN5 | `test_service.py::test_a_waiting_call_continues_on_submit_and_is_refused_on_decline`; AQ6, AQ7 | passed (live) |
| CB11 | **A connector keeps its tools while it needs setup.** A connection with no saved sign-in now mounts (the eager raise in `_oauth_provider_from_config` is deleted) and its first request raises the typed `MCPSignInNeeded` (`tools/mcp_sign_in.py`, reason `mcp_sign_in_needed`), replacing the old string (AQ51). Each live listing of a connector is kept (`connectors/listing.py`, the user cache folder; tool definitions carry no credential) and served with the typed trace `connector_tools_from_last_listing` when a listing fails because the connector needs setup (QC1 widens the test) | conn-be; CN5 | `tests/test_gact/test_mcp_sign_in.py`; AQ6 | passed (live) |
| CB12 | **`set_connector_value`.** A built-in in the core plugin (`gact/connector_tool.py`), deferred like other built-ins, passing the session's permission layers (`native_gate`). Connector and key match by id or name/label. Typed refusals: `connector_secret_not_agent_settable`, `connector_sign_in_not_a_value`, a need not `agent_settable`, an unknown or disabled connector (listing what the agent can set). Row "Set <need> of <connector>"; expansion shows the value and the state after it, or the refusal. Its signature became `values: {key: value}` in H6 *Amended (CS-D1):* unchanged; a disabled connector is still refused, and an enabled one the conversation has not added is not in the agent's tools | conn-be; CN6 | `test_service.py::test_agent_and_person_refusals`, `::test_the_tool_row_shows_the_value_or_the_refusal`; AQ8, AQ9 | passed (live) |
| CB13 | **Custom connectors.** `POST /v1/connectors {url}` makes a `custom` entry in `connectors.yaml` with an `address` need holding what was typed, plus a `sign-in` need when the server answers a ping with 401 and names protected-resource metadata. Enabled for all agents. `{command, args}` makes one with no needs. `DELETE /v1/connectors/{id}` removes a custom one | conn-be; CN1 | AQ11 | passed (live) |
| CB14 | **Secrets stay off every record.** The secret reaches the server only in environment or headers, filled after `${VAR}` expansion (`connectors/materialize.py::connector_spec`); `MCPServerSpec.env` is `repr=False` like its headers; a row says only `has_value`; the respond body's `values` is `repr=False` and never stored on the interaction | conn-be; CN8 | AQ5 grep | passed (live) |

### Display modes, the tab and lifetime (MA1 to MA7)

| ID | Decision | Source | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MA1 | CLIO advertises `availableDisplayModes: ['inline', 'fullscreen']` (`AVAILABLE_DISPLAY_MODES`, `web/src/components/clio/mcp-app-bridge.ts`); a surface placement advertises `['inline']` (an app in an A2UI surface cannot move). `fullscreen` is the workbench tab kind `mcp-app` (`McpAppTabTarget`, id `mcp-app:<appInstanceId>`) carrying `appInstanceId`, `dataRef`, `sessionId`, `resourceUri`, `sourceServer`, `toolName` and `label`. The label is the tool's title: the observer copies the definition's `title` (or `annotations.title`) onto the `mcp_app` part (`_tool_title` in `gact/mcp_apps.py`), the tool name is the fallback | host; plan MA1 | `mcp-app-host-features.test.tsx`; AQ13 | passed (live) |
| MA2 | `ui/request-display-mode` resolves in `resolveDisplayMode`: `fullscreen`/`inline` move the frame when the view declared that mode (draft: the host MUST NOT switch to a mode absent from `appCapabilities.availableDisplayModes`, if set); `pip`, an undeclared mode and any request from a surface answer with the current mode. The answer is posted before the move. The card has "Open in tab"; the tab has "Return to conversation", shown only when the app is its conversation's latest (MA5); closing that tab is Return. After a move the new frame's `ui/initialize` carries the new mode; a tab also sends `ui/notifications/host-context-changed` on every resize, with fixed `{width, height}` (a card: `{width, maxHeight: 720}`) | host; plan MA2; plan defaults "Display modes offered", "pip requests" | `mcp-app-host-features.test.tsx`; AQ13, AQ14 | passed (live) |
| MA3 | Exactly one frame per app. The placement lives in the live-set store; the card renders the in-tab row (`data-mcp-app-in-tab`, "Open in a tab", Show tab) while the app is in a tab. `McpAppSurface` keys its instance on placement, so a move unmounts one frame and mounts the other, which replays `ui/initialize`, tool input and tool result. A frame whose descriptor's `display_mode` disagrees with its placement asks its owner: the card yields to the tab, the tab claims the frame | host; plan MA3; plan default "State on moving a view" | AQ14: `[data-mcp-app-iframe]` count 1 after each move | passed (live) |
| MA3-keepalive | Keep-alive is force-mounting, not a DOM registry: an `mcp-app` tab's `TabsContent` is force-mounted and hidden (`data-[state=inactive]:hidden`; display none keeps an iframe's document alive), and stays mounted while the canvas is collapsed on desktop. A registry that re-parents a frame cannot keep an iframe alive: moving an iframe reloads it in every browser without `moveBefore`, which WebKitGTK (Tauri on Linux) lacks. **Its note that maximizing or restoring the canvas and closing the phone sheet remount the panel is superseded by MA3-maximize** | host | AQ23: `#loaded` unchanged after showing the browser tab and returning | superseded in part by MA3-maximize |
| MA3-maximize | **Maximizing or restoring the canvas no longer reloads app views** (QG-H1, Q-O4). The canvas had moved into another host with `createPortal` (`workbench.tsx`, `use-workbench-maximize.ts`), and re-parenting an iframe reloads it: Burette's selection and camera were lost on Restore while `mcp_app_context` still held the selection. The canvas now stays in place and restyles: `fixed` over its section's measured box (`useMaximizedSectionBounds`), or `fixed inset-0` in the phone sheet or window. Escape restores through its own `DismissableLayer`, mounted only while maximized. On a phone the closed canvas sheet stays mounted (Radix `forceMount`, hidden and `inert`) while an App tab is open, so closing and reopening the sheet no longer reloads the view | host (integrator, from q-grounds QG-H1 and q-apps Q-O4) | `workbench-portal.test.tsx`; AQ-maximize | passed (live) |
| MA4 | `PUT /v1/sessions/{sid}/mcp-apps/{app_id}/display-mode {mode}` sets `record.display_mode` and returns `{display_mode, pinned}` (unknown mode 422, wrong capability 404). The descriptor returns `display_mode` and `pinned`; the `mcp_app` part carries the mode it was made in. A reloaded UI restores the tab from the workbench's saved tabs; a card whose descriptor says `fullscreen` shows the in-tab row instead of mounting. The route also records the mode on the saved call (RO-D7) | host; plan MA4 | `test_display_mode_route_sets_mode_and_pin` | tests only |
| MA5 | `use-active-mcp-app.ts` is deleted; `mcp-app-live-set.ts` (`useMcpAppLiveSetStore`) holds every known app with `placement: inline \| tab \| surface \| ended` and, per session, the latest `mcp_app` block. The live inline app is that latest block; when it changes, the previous app is closed only if its placement is `inline`. An older app's tab has no card to return to, so Return or close closes it (MA7). `activeMcpAppId` prop drilling is removed; cards read `useMcpAppPresence`, turns group apps from `useShownMcpAppIds`. gact-tui issue #324's line "switching surfaces leaves the App frame DOM-absent" is amended here to apply to inline apps only; nothing was changed on GitHub | host; plan MA5 | `conversation.test.tsx`, `mcp-app-host-features.test.tsx`; AQ15 | passed (live) |
| MA6 | `MCPAppRecord.pinned` is `display_mode == "fullscreen" or surface_pinned`. `_expire_locked` skips pinned records and admission counts only unpinned ones. The limit and expiry are configuration: `gact.mcp_apps.registry_limit` / `CLIO_MCP_APP_REGISTRY_LIMIT` (64) and `gact.mcp_apps.registry_ttl_s` / `CLIO_MCP_APP_REGISTRY_TTL_S` (3600), in `config.defaults.yaml`, `docs/ENVIRONMENT.md`, `.env.example` and `scripts/config_key_notes.py`; the knob-audit rows (A:74, B:402–403) are retired. A cleanup-bearing record keeps its never-expire rule | host; plan MA6; plan defaults "Registry rules" | `test_pinned_records_never_expire_and_are_not_counted`, `test_registry_knobs_resolve_through_config`; AQ16, AQ17 | passed (live) |
| MA6-surface | A surface pins with `PUT …/pin {pinned}`, not with `display-mode`, so a reloaded UI never opens a tab for an app that lives in a surface (`registerMcpAppSurface` / `releaseMcpAppSurface`) | host; contracts | `test_display_mode_route_sets_mode_and_pin` | tests only |
| MA7 | Closing goes through the existing `DELETE …/mcp-apps/{app_id}` (`claim_close`, `_cleanup_record`, `finish_close`): closing the tab of an app that is not its conversation's latest, a surface unmounting, a newer inline app replacing an inline one. Session deletion (`cleanup_session_mcp_apps`) and shutdown (`cleanup_all_mcp_apps`) are unchanged; the cleanup functions now live in `gact/mcp_app_cleanup.py` (FX-D10) | host; plan MA7 | `mcp-app-host-features.test.tsx` ("closes an older app whose tab leaves"); AQ25 | passed (live) |
| FX-D9 | **A view whose server's fleet closed is not live** (q-pv H1). `gact/mcp_app_fleet.py` stamps each admitted record with a weak reference to the executor whose server opened it; `_expire_locked` drops a record whose fleet is closed (reaped, restarted, evicted for a configuration change) with the typed reason `mcp_app_server_stopped`, so the card's liveness check and the tab's mount answer "not live" and the card offers Reopen (MA19), which starts the server again. An unstamped record is never dropped on a guess; the drop is seen at the next registry access. **Decision: the fleet is not kept alive for an open view.** A viewed conversation already holds its fleet (`view_fleet`), and the reap happens only after nobody viewed it for the TTL, so no visible view is lost (rule 6). A first version that held the fleet for every registry record (the "reaper lease" option) was removed. Plan amended (MA7) | fix (integrator, from q-pv `sess_e6c2fcb24b2b`, `sess_2ed3819fecd3`) | `tests/test_gact/test_mcp_app_fleet.py`; FQ7 | passed (live) |
| FX-D10 | Every server-side close publishes the transient session event `mcp_app_closed {session_id, app_instance_id, reason}` (`server_stopped`, `expired`, `closed`, `session_ended`) through the registry's `on_closed` (`mcp_app_cleanup.publish_closed`); gact-tui's `dispatchMcpAppClosedFrame` marks the app ended (`markMcpAppEnded`), so its card offers Reopen and its tab says it ended (q-apps Q-O1). `mcp_app_closed` and `mcp_app_tool_call` join gact-tui's event vocabulary and `contract/SPEC.md`; `mcp_apps.py` stays at 726 lines | fix (q-apps Q-O1) | `test_mcp_app_fleet.py::test_every_server_side_close_is_announced`, `::test_the_close_notice_is_a_transient_session_event`; gact-tui `mcp-app-closed-event.test.ts`, `spec_vocabulary.test.ts` | tests only |
| MA-phone | Under the split width the canvas is a sheet over the conversation; when an app returns to its card (Return, the view asking for `inline`, or its tab closing) the sheet slides away so the card shows (`useMcpAppTabSync`, `SPLIT_WORKBENCH_QUERY`) | host (found at AQ14, 390×844) | `host-phone-4-returned.png` | passed (live) |
| MA-phone-dock | **On a phone an App tab docks between the conversation's header and its composer** (ctx-tools CT-N1, q-apps AQ31 and Q-X1: the sheet covered the composer). The phone sheet is non-modal, and while the active tab is an App it spans from the header's bottom to the composer's top (`phone-app-dock.ts`, ResizeObserver, `useWorkbenchAppTabs`); tapping the composer does not dismiss it. It is non-modal always: flipping a Radix dialog's modality while open remounts its content and produced "Maximum update depth exceeded" as the app returned to its card. ### The app's context and app tools (MA8 to MA14)

| ID | Decision | Source | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MA8 | One function builds a live app's block, `app_context_block` (core reminder `clio.reminder.mcp_app_context`, `plugins/builtin/core/ai.iowarp.clio/injections/mcp_app_context.md`): the server, the opening tool, the context the view last reported, and the view's own tools by agent name. Both paths call it: `post_mcp_app_message` (the view's `ui/message`) and the turn's working state (`working_state_injections` source `mcp_app_context`). Since CM1 the block also says which views are on screen and that `look_at_view` can see them | ctx-tools; plan MA8 | `test_a_turn_carries_one_block_per_live_app_and_a_closed_app_none`; AQ18 | passed (live) |
| MA8-D1 | Where the block appears: `turn_start_offloop._prepare_turn_off_loop_steps` puts the working-state injections into the turn; the agent loop records each as a `user` segment (`actor: "algorithm"`, `source: "mcp_app_context"`) ahead of the person's message in the ARC working set, and emits it to the transcript as an injection. As working state it is recorded only when its text changed, re-offered after a compaction and never summarized. A turn started by the view's own `ui/message` carries the block twice the first time; accepted, not deduplicated | ctx-tools | AQ18 | done |
| MA8-D3 | The block names the view's own tools, so the agent knows they exist without the system prompt changing: the deferred entry in the system prompt is shared by every session of an agent and cached as one block | ctx-tools; MA12 | AQ20 | done |
| CT-D3 | The block carries the whole report, `content` and `structuredContent`, as JSON, so the agent sees every key a view reports (answers a2ui's A2-F5) | ctx-tools (integrator) | AQ18 transcript | done |
| MA9 | The view's last report is stored in the session's clio-core record (fact MA9); an unchanged report (same sha256) writes nothing; the write runs off the event loop. Closing the app removes its entry and its tools (`forget_closed_app`). Owner module `gact/mcp_app_context.py`; the PUT route is in `gact/mcp_app_tool_routes.py`. `MCPAppRecord.model_context`, `model_context_digest` and `model_context_bytes` are deleted | ctx-tools; plan MA9; plan default "Where reported context and the saved call live" | AQ19 | tests only |

| MA12 | Each offered tool is a deferred tool `app__<server>__<tool>` built through `native_tool` (`gact/mcp_app_tools.py`), owned by `<server>` in the deferred catalog, so `search_tools` and `@<server>` (`summon.expand_server_summons`) load it; placed at build and every step (`agent_tool_refresher`, `sync_deferred_catalog` → `place_app_tools`). A tool the view drops leaves the catalog and stays callable unlisted so a late call is answered. **A loaded tool joins the tool list when the prompt is next rebuilt** (as `@server` loads do, summoning ledger DS6), not at the next compaction; until then the context block names it (plan amended). When two live views of one server offer the same name, the last to register wins. A name that is not a valid model tool name is skipped with `mcp_app_tool_name_invalid` | ctx-tools; plan MA12; plan default "App tool names" | `test_view_tools_join_and_leave_the_deferred_catalog`; AQ20, AQ22 | passed (live) |

A surface that mounts while a call waits gets it again; a repeated event runs the tool once. No timeout: the wait checks the turn's cancel every 250 ms. Permission is the native gate keyed by the `app__` name | ctx-tools; plan MA13; plan default "Timeout on an open view" | `test_a_call_reaches_the_view_and_returns_its_text`; AQ20 | passed (live) |

### Links, downloads and host capabilities (MA15 to MA17)

| ID | Decision | Source | Evidence | Status |

| MA15 | `ui/open-link` accepts `http:` and `https:` only (`checkedLink`), opens the URL with `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/browser/pages` and reveals it through `OPEN_BROWSER_PAGE_EVENT`. Anything else is `{code: -32000, data: {reason: "unsupported_scheme", scheme}}`; a malformed URL is `invalid_url`. Unknown view methods answer `-32601` with `data.reason: "unsupported_method"` | host; plan MA15; F1 | `mcp-app-host-features.test.tsx`; AQ23 | passed (live) |
| MA16 | `ui/download-file` (F2). `POST …/mcp-apps/{app_id}/downloads` (`gact/mcp_app_downloads.py`) saves each embedded resource into the session's workspace folder under the sanitized last segment of `resource.uri`, never overwriting (`name (2).ext`), and mints an artifact (`mint_artifact_outcome`, mechanism `harness`, producer tool `mcp_app.download`, `producing=False`). The view receives `{files: [{name, path, artifact_id}]}`; the UI shows a toast with Open. Typed refusals: `invalid_content`, `download_too_large` (`gact.mcp_apps.download_max_bytes`, 64 MiB), `no_workspace`, `download_name_taken`, and `download_link_unsupported` for a ResourceLink, because fetching an address a sandboxed view chose from CLIO's process would let the view reach any host on the person's network. No confirmation dialog: the file lands in the workspace, not on the person's disk, and is listed in Artifacts | host; plan MA16; draft spec | `test_download_saves_into_the_workspace_and_registers_an_artifact`, `test_download_without_a_workspace_folder_is_refused`, `test_safe_file_name`; AQ24 | passed (live) |

Values are CLIO's tokens resolved for the theme in use, resent on every switch. Mapping in `MCP_APP_STYLE_SOURCES` (`web/src/components/clio/mcp-app-host-styles.ts`): backgrounds from `--background`, `--card`, `--muted`, `--invert`, and 14 % of each status token over `--background`; text from `--foreground`, `--muted-foreground`, `--faint`, `--invert-foreground` and the status foregrounds; borders and rings from `--border`, `--input`, `--ring`, `--invert` and the status tokens; `--font-sans`/`--font-mono` from `--typeface-ui`/`--typeface-mono`; weights 400 to 700; Tailwind text sizes; radii from `--radius`; shadows from `--shadow-color`. A token a theme lacks is left out, so the view keeps its own fallback. The fixture views read the variables. ### A view's service and the sandbox host (MA23)

| MA23 | A server declares `_meta["ai.iowarp.clio/service"] = {port, token}` in the result that opens the view. `MCPAppRegistry.register` takes it off the stored result (`split_service_declaration`), so the descriptor, the replayed result, the transcript and the model never hold port or token. `/v1/sessions/{sid}/mcp-apps/{app_id}/service/{data_ref}/…` proxies HTTP (httpx) and WebSocket upgrades (websockets) to `127.0.0.1:<port>`. Owner modules `gact/mcp_app_service_declaration.py`, `gact/mcp_app_service.py`, `gact/mcp_app_service_proxy.py` | service; plan MA23 | `tests/test_gact/test_mcp_app_service.py` (26); AQ30, AQ31 | passed (live) |
| MA23-D1 | The capability travels as a path segment, `…/service/{data_ref}/`: relative URLs and a WebSocket built from `location` keep it; a cookie would need `SameSite=None` and leak across apps on one origin. The slashless form redirects; services must use relative URLs, and a redirect to an absolute path or the service's own loopback address is rewritten under the route | service; contracts | `test_http_through_the_route_carries_the_token_and_strips_the_browser` | tests only |
| MA23-D2 | The token reaches the service as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` on every proxied request and upgrade; the browser's `Authorization`, `Cookie`, `Origin`, `Referer` and the service's `Set-Cookie` and `Access-Control-*` are dropped | service; MA23 | same test | tests only |
| MA23-D3 | "A server CLIO launched": the view's source namespace must have a `stdio` spec in the session executor's `_clio_namespace_specs`; a URL server is refused `service_server_not_launched` (cached on the record), CLIO's own port `service_port_is_clio`, a malformed declaration `service_declaration_invalid`. CLIO always dials `127.0.0.1` | service; MA23 | `test_a_server_clio_did_not_launch_gets_no_service`, `test_unreachable_service_and_clio_port_are_typed`, `test_a_malformed_declaration_is_refused_with_a_reason` | tests only |
| MA23-D4 | The service URL is on the app's sandbox origin, never the UI's; the view gets it in the descriptor (`service_url`) and in `hostContext["ai.iowarp.clio/service"] = {url}`, checked by the host to be this app's own route | service; contracts | `mcp-app-service.test.ts`; `test_descriptor_gives_the_service_url_and_never_the_token`; AQ30 | passed (live) |
| MA23-D5 | The sandbox CSP drops every loopback source a resource declares (`127.0.0.0/8`, `localhost`, `*.localhost`, `::1`, `0.0.0.0`) and adds only the app's service route to `connect-src` and `frame-src`; each refused source is in the descriptor's `csp_refused` and recorded as `csp_loopback_source_refused` | service; MA23 "a view never reaches 127.0.0.1" | `test_csp_refuses_loopback_and_allows_only_the_service_route`, `test_mcp_v2_conformance.py` | tests only |
| MA23-D6 | A view frame without `allow-same-origin` sent `Origin: null`, so service responses answered `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: null`, an outermost middleware answered preflights for service paths, and the origin guard skipped its state-changing-method check there. **Superseded by MA-sandbox:** the view frame now has `allow-same-origin` on its server's own sandbox host, where the service route is too, so its requests carry that origin | service | `test_http_through_the_route_carries_the_token_and_strips_the_browser` | superseded by MA-sandbox |

A WebSocket checks the record on each frame. No read timeout or keep-alive ping on an open connection (rule 6); only connecting has a 10 s limit | service; MA23 | `test_route_is_bound_to_its_own_app_and_closes_with_it`; AQ31 | passed (live) |
| MA23-D8 | A declaration in a result the view gets through `tools/call` is removed and ignored (`service_declaration_ignored`) | service; rule 5 | `test_a_view_called_tool_cannot_hand_the_view_a_declaration` | tests only |
| MA23-D9 | Fixture `tests/fixtures/mcp_app_service_server.py` (FastMCP stdio, hand-written view, no Node build): a token-guarded loopback Starlette service (counter over a WebSocket, `GET state`, `POST bump`, a page with its own WebSocket); port and token fixed by `CLIO_SERVICE_FIXTURE_PORT` / `CLIO_SERVICE_FIXTURE_TOKEN` for qualification | service | AQ30 | done |
| MA23-D10 | Typed reasons (`SERVICE_REASONS`): `service_declaration_invalid`, `service_server_not_launched`, `service_port_is_clio`, `service_not_declared`, `service_unreachable` (502, or WebSocket close 1011), `service_declaration_ignored`, `csp_loopback_source_refused`, `mcp_app_closed`; each logged, written to the stream audit and kept in a queryable ring (`recorded_service_reasons`) | service; no silent fallback | `test_route_is_bound_to_its_own_app_and_closes_with_it` | tests only |
| MA-sandbox | **The view is same-origin with a sandbox host of its own** (F12). `_sandbox_url` names `s<sha256(server)[:16]>.mcp-apps.localhost:<CLIO port>` (`gact/mcp_app_sandbox_origin.py`): one host per originating server, so one server's views share storage and different servers are isolated; browsers resolve `*.localhost` to loopback without DNS. On a sandbox host `OriginGuardMiddleware` (`sandbox_host_refusal`, before the bearer check) serves only the sandbox document and the service route and refuses everything else `403 sandbox_origin_refused`; the sandbox route refuses any host but its app's (`sandbox_host_mismatch`). The inner frame gets `allow-scripts allow-same-origin allow-forms`, as the spec and `basic-host` do; the proxy forwards view messages whose origin is its own as well as `null`. `_alternate_loopback_origin` is deleted. Risk: WebKitGTK (Tauri on Linux) resolves `*.localhost` only where the system resolver does (systemd's nss-myhostname); not reachable from a cloud session. Plan amended (MA23) | host (integrator, from grounds F12); spec "Rendering the MCP App" | `test_mcp_apps.py`, `test_mcp_app_service.py`; AQ-sandbox | passed (live) |
| MA23-remote | **A CLIO reached over the network gets a configured sandbox host.** For a request on a non-loopback name the sandbox host comes from `gact.mcp_apps.sandbox_host` / `CLIO_MCP_APP_SANDBOX_HOST`: `*.apps.example.lan` gives `s<digest>.apps.example.lan` per server (needs wildcard DNS), a plain name gives all servers one host (no per-server storage isolation). A configured name that is loopback or in `gact.allowed_hosts` is ignored with a logged reason; with no usable name the descriptor answers `409 sandbox_host_unconfigured` and the card says what to set. The sandbox document accepts an embedder in `gact.allowed_hosts` or `gact.cors.origins`, and `BearerAuthMiddleware` admits without a token only the sandbox document and service route on a sandbox host (a frame cannot send the bearer; the capability in the URL binds them) | host (integrator); plan MA23 "wherever the browser is" | `test_a_remote_browser_needs_a_configured_sandbox_host`, `test_a_remote_browser_gets_a_per_server_sandbox_host`, `test_the_sandbox_route_needs_no_bearer_but_the_api_on_its_host_does`; AQ-remote (stand-in) | passed (live, stand-in) |
| FX-D11 | **A dead service says so.** The proxy answers a page load it cannot serve (`Sec-Fetch-Mode: navigate`, or `Accept: text/html`) with a readable "Service unavailable" page (message and reason) at the same status; a script's fetch still gets the JSON envelope. The descriptor probes the service port (one bounded loopback connect) and reports `service_refusal: service_unreachable`, so the header reads "Service unavailable" instead of "Ready", and the refusal line stays visible above a tab's frame (q-pv H1) | fix (integrator) | `test_unreachable_service_and_clio_port_are_typed`; `mcp-app-surface.test.tsx`; FQ8 | passed (live) |

The node half (`paraview_mcp/node/`) runs in `pvpython` and imports ParaView only inside functions, so the front ships it as data. Version 3.0.0 in `mcp-server-versions.toml`, `clio-analysis` bundle 1.1.0, new tags, doctor note, README, setup, marketplace docs, codex eval case, the simulation-visualize skill, website overview; generated files regenerated. | PV3 | Attaching. No address: the node half starts with the `pvpython` that `bash -c '<prepare>; command -v pvpython'` finds. An address: one control-master `ssh -o BatchMode=yes -o ControlMaster=yes -o ControlPersist=no -N [-J via] host` with the person's keys, agent and `~/.ssh/config` (`-J via` only when `ssh -G host` shows no `ProxyJump`); every script runs over that master in `bash -lc` (so Lmod's `module` works) and the one forward is `ssh -O forward -L 127.0.0.1:L:127.0.0.1:R`. The local port is kept across re-attaches, so the view's service route stays valid. Typed ssh failures: `interactive_sign_in`, `key_rejected`, `host_key_unknown`, `unknown_host`, `unreachable`, `ssh_failed`. OpenSSH runs a jump as a child `ssh -W`, so two `ssh` processes stand for one connection | pv-server; plan PV3 | `test_ssh_failures_are_typed`; AQ38, AQ39, AQ43 | passed (live) |
