### Looking at views and comments (the plan's addendum, CM1 to CM8)

| ID | Decision | Source | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CM1 | `look_at_view(view)`: a native tool (`gact/mcp_app_look.py`), deferred by the core plugin, withheld with `model_lacks_image_input` like `view_image`. It picks a live view by title, server, tool or id (the only one, or the only one on screen, when empty) and works as an app tool call: transient event `mcp_app_look {call_id, max_side: 1568}` to the surface holding the view, snapshot posted to `POST …/looks/{call_id}`, no timeout, cancel ends it, a closing app settles it as `mcp_app_view_closed`. `PUT …/shown {holder, shown}` says which mounts hold a view (a card and a tab overlap while a frame moves). A view no mount holds, or a conversation no window is subscribed to (`EventBus.subscriber_count`), is refused at once (`mcp_app_view_not_shown`, naming how to show it), and a look also ends that way when the last window goes while it waits: reality, not a timer. The model gets the native image part, then the identity, the view's last report and the snapshot's gaps (`agents/recorded_media.py`); the PNG is kept as a session snapshot and an artifact. Presentation: "Look at view" on the view's title, the image link, the report, the gaps; refusals are failed rows | comments (integrator re-scope); plan rule 6, MA8, MA9, MA13 | `tests/test_gact/test_mcp_app_look.py` (7); CM-Q1, CM-Q3 | passed (live) |
| CM2 | The pixels come from CLIO's own outer sandbox document, not the view and not a server re-render: the view frame is on its sandbox origin with `allow-same-origin` (MA-sandbox), so the sandbox document is same-origin with it and with frames it nests from that origin (trame through the service route). It answers `clio/view-snapshot` itself (`gact/mcp_app_snapshot_script.py`, in `_SANDBOX_DOCUMENT`); the view never receives it. It paints backgrounds, visible `canvas`, `img`, `video`, `svg` and one-line text, clipped per frame. A WebGL canvas with `preserveDrawingBuffer: false` reads blank after compositing, so during a snapshot its draw calls are wrapped, the frame shrinks 1 px for two frames, and the canvas is copied after the app's drawing task; then the wrappers are removed. Mol* keeps its buffer; trame-vtk sets `preserveDrawingBuffer:!1`. Typed gaps `frame_cross_origin`, `webgl_frame_not_redrawn`, `cross_origin_pixels`, `not_drawable`, `wrapped_text_not_drawn`; refusals `view_not_readable`, `view_not_laid_out`, `snapshot_tainted`, `snapshot_failed`. The spec has no screenshot request (the draft lists screenshot APIs as future work, `apps.mdx` 2411, 2460) | comments; plan rule 4 | probe (nested on-demand WebGL frame, 67 ms); `test_the_sandbox_document_answers_snapshots_itself`; CM-Q1 (vtk.js), CM-Q3 (Mol*) | passed (live) |
| CM2-D1 | No automatic "better path": CLIO does not call a server's image tool (ParaView's `capture`) in place of the snapshot. Which tool renders what is the server's knowledge, not data CLIO holds; the agent may still call it. A connector-entry field naming a render tool was considered and left out | comments; superseding principle 1, plan rule 3 | — | done |
| CM3 | Comments on app views. `McpAppSurface` takes `comments`; the card, the `mcp-app` tab and `clio.mcp-app.v1` set it, so every frame has the one Comments icon (`FrameCommentsIcon`). `comments/mcp-app-comments.tsx` runs one region-only `CommentSessionProvider` (destination `attachment`, kind `mcp-app`) with `McpAppRegionLayer`: a drag marks a region, a click marks a point (a 64 px box). Each mark takes the CM2 snapshot at 2x and copies the view's last report into `anchor.source.context`; a refused snapshot is a note on the mark. The send attaches the overview (marks drawn, at most 1568 px) and a crop per mark. Server: `documents/app_view_comments.py`, registered for kind `mcp-app`, renders per mark the view's identity, the box in percent and pixels, the point, the view's report at that moment (shared reports not repeated) and typed notes; no anchor JSON. Marks stay pinned while the session is open | comments; comments plan rules 1 to 3 | `test_app_view_comments.py` (3); `mcp-app-look.test.ts` (3); CM-Q2 to CM-Q4 | passed (live) |
| CM4 | Comments stay on what the screen shows; a mark carries the view's report as context only, and the view is not driven back to it | comments; comments plan "Out of scope" | — | done |
| CM5 | Route count guard `EXPECTED_ROUTE_METHOD_PAIRS` 467 → 469 for the two look routes (QG-F4 had set 467 for the apps streams' 16 pairs, all in owner modules; `app.py` unchanged from the base) | comments, q-grounds QG-F4 | `test_decomposition_guardrails.py` passes | done |
| CM6 | Found live: a WebGL wrapper applied as `original.apply(this, …)` threw `Illegal invocation` and blanked the vtk.js scene (now applies to the context); one look handler per view was lost when a card unmounted after its tab took over (now a stack per view, mirrored by the server's holder list); the snapshot was written in a different workspace key than the history reads (now in the call's own tool workspace, as `view_image` does) | comments | `test_an_older_mount_unloading_does_not_hide_a_newer_one`, `mcp-app-look.test.ts`; `cm-pv4`, `cm-bu2` (`sess_f835a9a07d1f`), `sess_265cc42bd2f8` | passed (live) |
| CM7 | After merging the integration branch: (a) app-tool handlers are a stack per view, as look handlers are (supersedes F-2; new test "keeps the older mount answering when the newer mount of the App stops first"); (b) `mcp-app-reopen.test.tsx`'s mock typed `as const`; (c) the snapshot paints each frame's own page background, else the host's themed background (MA-theme); (d) the look presenter shows the report as a code block; (e) showcase in `web/e2e/tool-operations.spec.ts`: a look with the image link, a 42-line report and a gap, a running and a refused look, at 1440 and 390 | comments (integrator) | `playwright test -c playwright.components.config.ts e2e/tool-operations.spec.ts`: 2 passed; `cm1-look-at-view-review-fixture-{1440,390}.png` read; vitest 13 files 84 tests; `tsc -b` clean | done |
| CM8 | A surface capture paints its app views and draws each chart once. Found by the final agent (`sess_cf269e667f23`): a dashboard (`clio.mcp-app.v1` ParaView + `clio.chart.v1`) checked with `capture_a2ui_surface` showed a blank ParaView body and a doubled chart. `labelledPng` clones the DOM with html-to-image, which cannot clone a sandboxed app frame, and it also cloned each canvas as an image before drawing the live canvas over it, offset. Canvases and app frames are now left out of the clone; canvases are drawn from their live pixels only; each app frame is painted at its box from the CM2 snapshot (`a2ui-app-capture.ts`, snapshots registered by frame in `mcp-app-look.ts`), or as a labelled box naming the typed reason; snapshot gaps are noted on the picture. Region comments over a surface use the same path. Snapshot fixes found on the way: the view frame is a flex item, so the redraw nudge narrows it with `max-width`; a blank WebGL canvas idle beside one that redrew is `webgl_canvas_idle` | comments (integrator finding) | `a2ui-app-capture.test.ts`; CM-Q6 | passed (live) |

### Connector discovery (the plan's addendum, CD-D1 to CD-D10)

IDs carry `CD-D` (decisions) and `CD-Q` (scenarios), apart from conn-ui's `CD1` to `CD7` contract rows above.

| ID | Decision | Source | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CD-D1 | **What the agent knows.** The session facts gain a piece, `session_facts_connectors` (core injection, `x_trigger: session_facts`), naming each connector that does not reach this conversation's agent: off, or on for other agents only. One line each, `Name (id): description`, then one grounding sentence (offer it with `offer_connector`, the person chooses). Owner `gact/connector_offer.py::offerable_facts`, called from `agents/composition.py::_inventory_lines`. Facts ride the conversation and are offered again when they change, so the cached system prompt stays shared and the list shrinks after a connector is turned on. At most `connectors.offer_listed` (`CLIO_CONNECTORS_OFFER_LISTED`, default 20) are named, with the total; `offer_connector` with no connector lists them all. A session whose agent cannot be resolved names none (typed trace `connector_offer_no_agent`). Grounding, not a handcuff: it says what exists and who decides (repo principle 3); no keyword matching decides anything *Amended (CS-D6):* the facts name every connector this conversation does not have, on or off, `Name (id, on|off): description`; `offerable` reads the session's resolution (`conversation_connectors`), not a scope | discover; plan rules 1 to 3 | `test_offer.py` (offerable, cap, render); `test_session_inventory_facts.py`, `test_reminders_working_state.py` unchanged; CD-Q1 to CD-Q5 ("its connector is off for me") | passed (live) |
| CD-D2 | **The tool.** `offer_connector(connector, reason)`, native, deferred in the core plugin (`defer=True`, loaded by `search_tools`; the agent also called it directly from the facts, CD-Q5). Unknown connector: `connector_not_found` with the offerable list; already reaching this agent: no card, `already_on`, and its tools are made sure in the running turn; no conversation: `connector_offer_no_conversation`; empty `connector`: the list. Otherwise it raises the card and waits | discover | `test_offer.py::test_listing_unknown_and_already_on` | passed (live) |
| CD-D3 | **One way to ask (plan rule 2, CN5).** The offer is the same pending interaction, kind `connector_setup`, with `intent: "enable"` and `description`, `reason`, `agent_ref`, `agent_name`, `asks_sign_in`, answered at the same respond route. `connector_setup.wait_for_card` is the one blocking wait both use: an event per card, no timeout (rule 6), stopping the turn ends it (`connector_offer_cancelled`, outcome `cancelled`). The key is `intent`, not `mode`: `mode` is already the elicitation payload's field in gact-tui's `PendingInteraction` type | discover; plan rule 2, rule 6 | `test_offer.py::test_stopping_the_turn_ends_the_offer`; `connector-offer.test.tsx` | passed (live) |
| CD-D4 | **The card.** `ConnectorOfferResponse` (`connector-offer-response.tsx`), chosen by `ConnectorSetupResponse` on `intent`: "Turn on <name>?", the logo (monogram without one), the name with its state in words ("Not enabled"), the description, "Why the agent asks" with the agent's reason, the required rows, the optional rows behind "Optional settings (N)" (ParaView's three are optional, so its card stays short), "It also needs a sign-in…" when it has one, an "Available in every conversation" switch, and Turn on / Not now. Its line in the conversation reads "Turn on <name>", waiting for you ("Choose below"), then "turned on" or "not now" | discover; CN3, CN4, gact-tui rules | `connector-offer.test.tsx` (5); shots in CD-Q1 to CD-Q6 | passed (live) |
| CD-D5 | **Scope (CN10).** CN10 has no "this conversation" scope: a connector applies to every conversation or to chosen agents. The card's switch therefore offers every conversation (default, `scope: "all"`) or, off, "Only <this conversation's agent> can use it" (`{agents: [existing…, agent_ref]}`). An already-everywhere connector stays `all`. The choice travels as `metadata.scope` (`all` / `agent`); anything else is `connector_scope_invalid` (422). The respond route now forwards `metadata` to the connector card *Amended (CS-D6):* superseded: the card has no switch and adds the connector to this conversation only; `metadata.scope` is not read and `connector_scope_invalid` is gone | discover; plan CN10 | `test_offer.py` (scope agent, bad scope); CD-Q2 (`{"agents":["clio/standard"]}`) | passed (live) |
| CD-D6 | **Turn on.** Values typed in the card are checked as Extensions checks them (`check_values`, by the person, so a secret is allowed); a required row still empty is 422 `connector_setup_incomplete` and the card stays; then `connectors_service.enable` (save, probe, plugin follows, open conversations re-pinned as a note, CN7, CN9). A sign-in row is not shown in the offer: the connection it signs in to exists only once the connector is on, so the first call asks for it through the CN5 card *Amended (CS-D6):* Add enables the connector only when it is off, then adds its plugin to this conversation's delta (`connector_follow.add_to_conversation`, the `note` mode); a conversation that cannot take it answers 422 with the typed reason | discover; CN5 to CN9 | `test_offer.py` (incomplete, secret kept out of the row and result, sign-in) | passed (live) |
| CD-D7 | **The same turn continues.** A running turn keeps the fleet it started with (#1244), so the enabled connector is not in it. After Turn on, `connector_offer.join_running_turn` mounts the connector's namespace in the turn's fleet (`connector_rebind.join_namespace`: a spec from the saved connection, a proxy installed as a rebind installs one, the enable's own listing merged, else a fresh listing) and has the turn's tool refresh take that namespace whole (`LiveToolRefresher.take`, created when the agent had none). The loop's next step lists the tools (`mcp_tools_reached_agent`). Failures are typed (`connector_join_no_turn`, `_fleet_unavailable`, `_no_connection`, `_unusable`, `_listing_failed`), returned as `join_reason`, and the tools then arrive with the next message *Amended (CS-D6):* unchanged mechanism; also used when the connector was already on | discover; plan CN10 ("its tools are usable at once") | `test_offer.py::test_join_running_turn_*`, `test_join_namespace_*`; trace `connector_joined_turn namespace=spice … tools=8 source=last_listing` then `mcp_tools_reached_agent added=spice_…` (CD-Q1) | passed (live) |
| CD-D8 | **A following plugin's skills** arrive with the plugin-change note at the next message, not in the same turn: the turn's `load_skill` was built from its skill runtime. The result names the plugin and says so; the same turn has the server's tools and, when known, the server's own instructions *Amended (CS-D6):* the result names `plugins` (the ids now in this conversation) | discover; CN9 | CD-Q2 (ParaView worked from the tools alone) | open (a same-turn skill would need the turn's skill runtime to take the joined plugin) |
| CD-D9 | **Not now** is the offer answered, not the tool failing: the result carries `refusal: "connector_offer_declined"` (not `error`), naming the connector, and the row keeps its success mark with Answer "Not now"; the card's line says "not now". A first version returned `error`, which the observer turned into a failed row ("1 failed") and, with a `denied` status, the permission wording "Denied, you denied it" | discover | `test_offer.py::test_not_now_*`; CD-Q4 (before), CD-Q5 (after) | passed (live) |
| CD-D10 | **Presentation** (`docs/tool-result-presentation.md`): the row reads "Offer <connector>" (subject block `connector`; while waiting `present_start` shows the requested id and the reason); expansion shows Why, Answer (Turned on / Already on / Not now), Where (Every conversation / Only <agent>), Tools (N) with every name, and a "Can't be reached" line when the connector did not answer; refusals are failed rows, a stopped wait a cancelled one; with no connector the row reads "List connectors to offer". Showcase in `test_offer.py::test_the_row_reads_offer_*` (30 tool names, a long connector name, every outcome) | discover | CD-Q1 row screenshot | passed (live) |

### Cross-cutting fixes

| ID | Decision | Source | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| FX-D1 | **In a GACT server process every sync MCP executor runs on the application loop** (FX-F1). `task_call_context.executor_loop()` returns the bound app's loop, or else the server's one serving loop (`loop_guard.server_loops()`, registered by the lifespan); outside a server (the CLI) the executor keeps a private loop. Built on the serving loop's own thread (blocking there would deadlock) or with several serving loops in one process (tests), it keeps a private loop and logs `mcp_executor_private_loop` (`built_on_server_loop_thread`, `several_server_loops`). The fix is at the executor, not at the callers, so a future builder with no app bound cannot bring the hang back. Fixes reopen's open hang, a2ui A2-F4 and q-apps Q-F3 | fix | `tests/test_tools/test_executor_loop.py` (5; the integration test fails at the base); FQ2, FQ3, FQ3b; AQ25 rerun (five replays, the parallel request) | passed (live) |
| FX-D3 | A connection replaced while busy (`_retire_superseded`) is kept until its work ends and marked; a failing call on it records `external_mcp_connection_superseded: <error>`. A replay ending with an uncertain receipt answers `call_failed` ("…whether it acted on the call is not known") instead of `view_not_opened` (FX-F2). Nothing is retried: an uncertain mutating call is never replayed | fix | `test_external_connection_superseded.py`, `test_mcp_app_open.py::test_every_failure_is_a_typed_reason_with_the_saved_call` | tests only |
| FX-D6 | **A view's own call never lands inside an open turn** (q-apps Q-F1: a surface's opening call split the agent's answer inside a code span and was recorded as the agent's call). Each `_run_bound` call runs inside `view_call_transcript.collecting()`, and `tool_observer._append_live_assistant_part(_once)` asks `view_call_transcript.hold()` first (two hook lines; `tool_observer.py` stays at 930). With a turn open the rows are held and written when the call ends as their own assistant message, `metadata.origin: "mcp_app"`; with none, the ordinary path | fix (integrator); clio-agent `1a81e8ede` | `test_view_call_transcript.py`; FQ4; AQ27 rerun at 390 | passed (live) |
| FX-D7 | `session_work` reads messages and schedules through `run_off_loop` (`_work_rows`), so a first read after a restart rehydrates off the loop thread (FX-F7) | fix (integrator; AQ26 and AQ47 need clean `failedRequests` after a restart) | `test_session_work_off_loop.py`; FQ6 | passed (live) |
| FX-D8 | Which executor an out-of-turn replay uses: `_run_bound` → `_bound_executor` → `ClioAgent._active_tool_executor()`, the workspace's cached executor whoever built it, rebuilt when missing, reaped or reconfigured. Its builders (commands listing, summon targets, warm-ups, task recovery, the reconnect route, the view call) all run off the serving loop's thread, so with FX-D1 each gets the serving loop; none logged `mcp_executor_private_loop` live, and the agent had 0 private executor loops after restarts, reopens, re-saves and turns | fix (integrator question, q-apps `sess_791744fadd33`) | code reading; FQ3 | done |
| FX-D4 | `tools.script_media_max` has an operator note in `scripts/config_key_notes.py`; references regenerated with `scripts/gen_env_reference.py` | fix | `test_env_reference.py`: 26 passed | done |
| FX-D5 | The two size-ratchet failures (`gact/agents/clio_react.py` 817 against baseline 804; `plugins/store.py` 846 against the 800 cap) are identical at the base and untouched by this campaign; left for their owners | fix FX-F4, q-grounds QG-Q1 | `check_file_size.py` on the base and on head | done |
| FX2-D1 | **A server declares its path arguments** in the tool definition's `_meta["ai.iowarp.clio/paths"]`: per argument `machine` (`local`, `remote` with an optional display `host`, or `none`) and `access` (`read`, `write`). On the definition, not a result, because provenance and the sandbox note need it for failed calls too. A malformed declaration is ignored with `path_declaration_invalid`. No service name in Python | fix2 (q-pv H3); contracts "A tool's path arguments" | `clio_agent/tools/tool_path_contract.py`; `test_declared_tool_paths.py` (18) | passed (live) |
| FX2-D2 | The execution boundary binds the called tool's declaration for the call (`tool_path_contract.bind_call`, a context variable keyed by the tool name), so provenance and sandbox readers never reach for an executor; it replaces a lookup already on the line, so `tools/execution.py` stays at 986 | fix2 | `test_a_successful_remote_open_is_not_shown_degraded` | done |

On success it is one `used` edge `remote:<host>:<path>`, evidence `schema-arg`, note `remote_input_not_observed`, nothing read or hashed; no `provenance_warnings`, so the row keeps the server's status. Declared `none`, local writes and remote writes leave the input scan; declared local reads join the external-input contract. The shell and `run_code` seams are unchanged (their `SandboxResult` says the command ran under the fence, and they read stderr for the denied path) | fix2 | — | done |
| QPV-F2 | **An MCP tool's image results reach the model.** The model had read only `[image image/png 1280x800]`. Each image block of a model-facing call is snapshotted into the workspace and joins the call's media slot, as `view_image` does (`tools/mcp_result_media.py`, one line in `tools/execution.py`, still 986); app and host calls attach nothing; an image that cannot be shown is logged with a typed reason | q-pv (AQ46, `sess_2ed3819fecd3`); clio-agent `a4804b6e2` | `test_mcp_result_media.py` (5), `test_model_media.py` (23), `test_execution.py` (48); AQ37 | passed (live) |
| QG-F1 | **A string-or-list parameter written as a JSON list is the list.** The `claude_code` provider's invoke protocol (`lm/engines/invoke_calls.py`) passed a parameter raw when its schema accepts a string, and ltspice-mcp's `signals: list[str] \| Literal["all"]` accepts `"all"`, so `["v(out)"]` reached the server as a string and was refused three times (`sess_a8ad078186b4`). `_value` now reads text that parses as a JSON array or object as that structure when the schema also takes one (or is untyped or a `$ref`); plain strings stay raw. | FN-D1 | **A connector says "Starting…" while Enable runs** (QG-S2, CN3). Enable and Turn off share a mutation key (`use-connectors.ts`, `useConnectorChanges`), so every place the connector appears sees the pending change. `connectorStatus(connector, change)` gives "Starting…" (running tone, "CLIO is starting it and checking what it needs.") or "Turning off…" in the card's state, its scope line, its button and its Extensions row (`extensionEntries({changes})`) | final | `connector-setup.test.tsx` "says Starting… in words while Enable runs", "reads Starting… in the Extensions list"; live `fin-aq35-enable` (`report.starting`: card "Starting…", ParaView row "Starting…"), `qg-s2-connector-starting-in-words-desktop.png` | passed (live) |
| FN-D2 | **Only the latest App lives inline** (Q-O2, MA5). A view reopened from an older card opens in its tab (`openMcpAppInTab`, fullscreen on the service), and a reopened view mounts in its card only when that card is the conversation's latest. This narrows RO-D12: a view reopened from an older card no longer mounts in that card | final; MA5, MA7, RO-D12 | `mcp-app-reopen.test.tsx` "reopens an older card into its tab, so only the latest App lives inline"; live `reopen_older.mjs` `sess_d954eb3b575c`: before `{inline: 1, tab: 0}`, after Reopen of the older card `{inline: 1, tab: 1}`, `q-o2-older-card-reopened-into-its-tab-one-inline-desktop.png` | passed (live) |
| FN-D3 | **A restored tab of a transcript App stays** (AQ47 note, MA19). Root cause: the workbench restores `mcp-app` tabs from `localStorage`, but the conversation's live set notes every transcript App as `inline` when the messages load; `useMcpAppTabSync` then read the restored tab as "left" and closed it before `McpAppRestoringTab` claimed it, and the latest card asked the service, found the view gone after the restart and ended it. A reopened view (AQ26's boards tab) is not in the transcript, which is why that tab survived. Fix: at mount, `useMcpAppTabSync` claims every restored tab's App (`claimRestoredMcpAppTab`) before any card notes it | final; MA19, RO-D11 | `mcp-app-reopen.test.tsx` "stays open after a restart when its App is a transcript card" (fails without the claim); live `paraview_flows/final_restart.json` `sess_d29eac082d09` "restored tab: mcp-app:tfFW…" by itself, and `final_colors_restart.json` `sess_4570eb29364a` | passed (live) |
| FN-D4 | **The connector setup card's line sits under the call it holds** (q-conn note, CN5). Root cause: the `connector_setup` interaction had no `source.invocation_id`, and the conversation links question lines to calls only by it (and only for questions). `await_setup` records `peek_call_identity()` as the row's `invocation_id`; `questionInteractionsForTool` includes `connector_setup`, and `splitWaitingQuestion` keeps it on its own line. While the card waits the call has no row yet (as with a permission), so the waiting state is the dock card and the session's attention mark; the line shows "Set up X" and its outcome under the call | final; CN5, CB10 | `test_service.py::test_the_card_names_the_call_it_holds`; `connector-setup.test.tsx` "puts its line under the call it holds"; live `sess_a1859a8cc410` (`connector_setup_line.mjs`): "Set up Fixture Secret declined", `cn5-connector-setup-line-under-its-call-declined-desktop.png` | passed (live) |
| FN-D5 | A need's help without a full stop gets one before "The agent can set this." (`connector-need-rows.tsx`); the fixture catalog's address help ends with one | final; q-conn note | `connector-setup.test.tsx` "ends help without a full stop with one" | done |
| FN-D6 | **`no_paraview` names the connector's values by key** (ground, not handcuff): "set `address` ('Where ParaView runs') …, `via` ('Jump host') …, `prepare` ('Command to run first') …, which the person or the agent can set" (clio-kit `paraview_mcp/errors.py`) | final (integrator's ask); AQ35 | `test_front.py::test_scene_tool_without_paraview_returns_the_message`; AQ35 rerun below | passed (live) |
| FN-D7 | **A volume is drawn on the ParaView machine.** The browser's renderer (trame-vtk's vtk.js) draws no ParaView `Volume` representation, so the dashboard's view was blank while the agent's capture showed the volume. `update_view_mode` goes remote for any visible `Volume` (unless the person pinned a mode) with `view.reason = "volume"`; the view's note says "Switched: a volume is drawn on the ParaView machine" and the agent's scene text "a volume is shown, which only ParaView draws" | final; PV view modes (AQ45) | `test_node_half.py::test_a_volume_is_drawn_on_the_paraview_machine` (pvpython `view_mode_check.py`), `test_front.py`; live `dash-hero3`/`dash-hero4` (manifest `{"mode": "remote", "reason": "volume"}`), before/after `dashboard-paraview-volume-blank-in-browser-before-fix-dark.png`, `dashboard-paraview-hero-dark-t0.3-before-scrub.png` | passed (live) |
| FN-D8 | **A restored scene keeps its saved camera** (FN-O3). Root cause: `paraview.simple.Render` resets the camera on a process's first render (`_funcs_internals.first_render`), so a node half started after a restart applied the manifest's camera and then the view's first render zoomed it to fit. The engine owns the camera (a new scene fits it, a restored one applies its own), so `Engine.__init__` calls `simple._DisableFirstRenderCameraReset()`. The manifest already saved position, focal point, view up, view angle, parallel scale and projection | final (integrator's ask) | `test_node_half.py::test_the_camera_comes_back_exactly_after_a_restart` (pvpython `camera_check.py`: build a moved, zoomed camera, restore in a fresh process, render once; failed before the fix); live `final_camera_restart.json` + `final_camera_after_restart.json`, `sess_0d662de1931f`: the manifest camera identical before and after `stack.sh restart`, the two hero shots identical (`fn-o3-camera-before-restart-hero-desktop.png`, `fn-o3-camera-after-restart-identical-hero-desktop.png`) | passed (live) |
| FN-D9 | **Chart actions reachable on touch** (FN-O4). In a narrow dashboard the chart's actions floated over its corner with no line of their own and were `invisible` on `hover:none` until the chart had focus, which a tap on the plot rarely gave. On touch they now keep a line under the title and stay visible (the SurfaceToolbar's own touch emphasis); only a hover-capable pointer folds them into the corner (`a2ui-chart-header.tsx`) | final (integrator's ask) | `a2ui-chart-header.test.tsx`; live 390×844 `--touch` (`hover: none`, `pointer: coarse` confirmed), new plain-request dashboard `sess_9a407a9efdb8`: More → "Select a time by keyboard" → 0.4, one `set_timestep`, the view at 5/12 (`fn-o4-dashboard-chart-actions-visible-on-touch-scrubbed-phone-dark.png`) | passed (live) |
| FN-D10 | **A declined setup names its call** (FN-O5). The typed `connector_declined` refusal now reads "Denied · <tool> · connector setup declined" (`withPermissionDecision`, `CONNECTOR_DECLINED`), and any denied call with no target names its tool on the row (`tool-compact-row.tsx`) | final (integrator's ask) | `tool-observability.test.tsx` "names the tool and the declined connector setup on a call with no target"; live `sess_cf69d2795549`: "Denied · Fixture Secret Check · connector setup declined" with "Set up Fixture Secret declined" under it (`fn-o5-declined-setup-row-names-tool-desktop.png`) | passed (live) |

### Stack fixtures

How the integrated qualification set up its stacks, so the step files can be rerun.

- **Connectors (AQ1 to AQ12):** source `scripts/cloud/checks/apps/connector-fixtures.env.sh` before `stack.sh start`; run `tests/fixtures/oauth_mcp_server.py --port <port>` for the sign-in fixture; add and trust `tests/fixtures/connectors/marketplace` with `POST /v1/plugins/marketplaces`. The shipped connectors are enabled in Settings > Extensions.
- **App fixtures (AQ13 to AQ31):** each saved with `PUT /v1/mcp/configuration/<name>` (stdio, `always_load: true`, the worktree's venv python):

  | Connection | Fixture | Env |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `hostpanel` | `tests/fixtures/mcp_apps/host_panel_server.py` | none |
  | `board` | `tests/fixtures/mcp_apps/view_tools_server.py` | none |

  AQ16 and AQ17 lower the registry in the stack's `config.yaml` (`gact: {mcp_apps: {registry_ttl_s: 60, registry_limit: 2}}`) and restart; remove it afterwards.
- **ParaView (AQ35 to AQ50):** the clio-agent venv runs the stream's clio-kit installed editable, with paraview-mcp 3.0.0 (3.1.0 for the pv-ui rows, which use `paraview_flows/panel_*.json`) (PV-N1); the `paraview` connector is enabled; the cluster is clio-kit `tests/fixtures/paraview-cluster/cluster.sh build`, `up` (`variant pvserver` for AQ40, `grow start` for AQ46), `scenarios 39 43 50`, `down`. Step files `paraview_flow.mjs` with `paraview_flows/*.json`, `paraview_surface.mjs`, `paraview_ssh.sh`, `paraview_token.sh`.
- **clio-schemas:** 0.7.0b2 installed editable for `clio.mcp-app.v1` (A2-F1).

### First-run journey (stack N=21, plain requests)

A first-time scientist on a clean configuration: stack N=21 (agent 8821, UI 5221) on `ws/journey` after merging the integration branch, a fresh `.cloud-run/xdg` (nothing enabled, no workspace), `CLIO_CLOUD_ISOLATE=1`, `CLIO_ARC_CTE_FILE_CAPACITY=128MB`. The default marketplace came from this branch's `external/clio-agent-marketplace` (`9235e14`, later `19528ec`), as a checkout installs it; an installed CLIO fetches the pinned `bc5ddb2` instead (JR-R1). Every request is plain and typed in the first screen's composer, as a person would; permission prompts are answered with **This session**, and every one is shot before it is answered. Step files: `journey.mjs` (requests, prompts, setup cards, record), `journey_enable.mjs` (Settings → Extensions → Enable, clicks counted), `journey_restart.mjs`, `journey_upgrade.py`; records in `.cloud-run/journey/*.json`. Screenshots (all read) are in `/home/user/wt/gallery/journey/`. The person's data was in a folder outside any workspace (`.cloud-run/sci-data/`: `wavelet.vti`, `pulse_series.pvd`, `rc_lowpass.cir`); the cluster was `paraview-cluster/cluster.sh up` under the prefix `clio-pv-journey`.

#### Remaining frictions, ranked by pain

| Rank | ID | Friction | Proposal |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | JR-P1 | **A connector that is off is invisible from the conversation.** JR1, JR3 and JR4 each ended in a workaround the person did not ask for (a GLB mesh, a `pvbatch` PNG, numbers without a plot, a PDB in ParaView), and `search_tools("paraview")` says only "No deferred tool matches". Finding Settings → Extensions is on the person | The `discover` stream's card. Today's exact behaviour is in JR1 to JR4 for its before/after |
| 2 | JR-P2 | **Connector values are global.** "Connect to node08" in one conversation re-pointed ParaView for every conversation: JR1's Reopen failed with `unknown_scene`, and a stale "Command to run first" from this machine ran on node08. Fixing one conversation breaks the other | A saved view keeps the connector values it was opened with, and Reopen offers "ParaView now runs on node08; open this on <this machine>?" Longer term, per-conversation agent-set values (the person's Save makes them global), or several named places per connector. `set_connector_value`'s result should list the values left from before |
| 3 | JR-P3 | **Five prompts for one 3D view.** Allowing a script for the session does not cover the ParaView calls inside it; each view-shaping call (camera, representation, filter) asks once | A connector-level "Allow ParaView for this session" on the card; mark view-only tools non-destructive (`destructiveHint: false`, `idempotentHint`) in clio-kit so a policy can treat them as low risk; or a per-entry default in the connector catalog |
| 4 | JR-R1 | **Release pins.** An installed CLIO pins `clio-kit==2.12.3` (the old 26-tool ParaView server, which ignores the connector's values) and fetches the marketplace at `bc5ddb2`, which has no `paraview` plugin, so the connector gets a skill-less carrier | Release clio-kit with paraview-mcp 3.1.0 and clio-schemas 0.7.0b2; move the gitlink and `DEFAULT_MARKETPLACE_COMMIT` to a marketplace commit with `19528ec`; add a test that every catalog connector's `plugin` exists in the pinned marketplace |
| 5 | JR-P5 | **Dashboards: a picture instead of the live view, guessed keys, no self-check.** The view's reported keys and channel tools are nowhere in data, and `inspect_a2ui_surface` reports `ready: false` until the person opens the panel | JR-F6 grounds ParaView. Generally: list each view tool's reported `structuredContent` keys (from the app's declared schema or `_meta`) where `a2ui_mcp_app_tool_not_found` lists view tools; let the agent render a surface it built without a person's viewer |
| 6 | JR-P6 | **"Ready" on a blank view.** Burette's narrow and phone viewer draws nothing while the frame reads Ready; maximizing does not reload it, so the documented "maximize the panel" does not help a view that already failed | Upstream one-line fix (QG-B1). In CLIO: a "Reload view" item in the frame's menu, and the apps doc to say "Return to conversation, widen, Open in tab" until then |
| 7 | JR-P7 | **Views are named by their tool's imperative title**: tabs "Open a ParaView view", "Preview a PDB structure", "Plot Waveforms"; "Could not reopen Open a ParaView view"; the frame's accessible name "Open a ParaView view interactive view" | Label a view by connector and subject ("ParaView: pulse_series.pvd"), from the view's own title (`ui/initialize`), the resource title or the dataset argument; keep the tool title for the call row |
| 8 | JR-P8 | **Files land where CLIO was started.** With no workspace, the agent's outputs (`wavelet_view/`, `rc_lowpass_out/`, `pdb_1crn/`, `reports/`) went into the launch folder (here the source checkout), and the SPICE server wrote `.ltspice-mcp/` and `ltspice-mcp.toml` there | A first-run default workspace under the person's data folder; connector servers start in their own state folder (a `cwd` in the entry) |

The integrator's two watch items: (1) no page error `Cannot read properties of null (reading '0')` appeared in these runs, including the maximized dashboard, comment and Burette hero shots (console empty in each except Burette's upstream lines); (2) the region-selection trap was not driven here: JR6 commented through the view's own Comments icon. In the JR6 dashboard shots the chart's toolbar is seven unlabelled icons beside the viewer's own comment and file actions, so a person who wants to mark across the view and the chart has no cue which control does it. Ranked with JR-P11. Proposal: one Comments entry on the dashboard frame that marks across components, with the chart's own control reading "Mark regions in this chart".

### Connector values per conversation (CV)

Stream `conn-values`, answering the first-run journey's JR-P2 and JR7: connector values were global, so "connect to node08" in one conversation broke another conversation's ParaView view on Reopen and carried this machine's "Command to run first" to node08. The person decided that values are **per conversation**. Plan CN6 and CN7 are *Amended* with the table of where each part of a connector lives; contracts "Connectors" *Amended*; site `connectors.mdx` and `sdk/connectors.mdx` *Amended*. Merged `ws/conn-scope` (connectors are added per conversation; enable = installed) in clio-agent and gact-tui.

#### Decisions

| ID | Decision | Where | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CV-D1 | **A conversation's own values are an overlay in its session metadata**, `connector_values: {<id>: {<key>: value}}`, for `address` and `value` needs only. Secrets stay in the credential store and sign-ins in the connection's sign-in, for every conversation. Session metadata reaches the client in full and is patchable (`PATCH /v1/sessions/{sid}`), so the overlay is **checked on every read** (`connectors/overlay.py` `validated`): unknown connector or need, a secret or sign-in key, a non-string, more than one line, `${`, longer than 4096, not one of a picker's options, or an address starting with `-` (an option to `ssh`) is dropped with the typed trace `connector_overlay_value_refused` (connector, key, reason; never the value). The same `value_problem` check is now the card's and Extensions' (`check_values`), so the address `-` refusal applies to defaults too | `connectors/overlay.py`, `gact/connector_values.py` `session_overlay`, `connectors_service.check_values` | `test_a_client_patched_overlay_is_checked_on_every_read` (a client-patched `-oProxyCommand=…` address, a secret, a sign-in, `${need.token}`, a newline, an over-long value, a non-mapping: all refused, none reaches a declaration), `test_the_same_check_refuses_an_option_injection_from_the_card` | done |
| CV-D2 | **The saved connection is a template**: `materialize.declaration(entry)` keeps `${need.<key>}` for every need. `plugins/servers.py` `server_declarations(..., connector_values=)` rebuilds a connector connection from its current entry (so a connection an older CLIO saved with values baked in follows the conversation too; its sign-in block is kept) and fills the conversation's overlay over the defaults (`fill_declaration`). A spec parsed with no conversation (`spec_from_declaration`: Extensions' probe, a rebind) fills what is left from the defaults; secrets are filled when the server starts, as before. `materialize.resolved(entry, values)` is the old baked form, for the probe and the sign-in storage key | `connectors/materialize.py`, `plugins/servers.py`, `tools/mcp_config.py` (+2 lines, docstring −2: still 800) | `test_the_saved_connection_is_a_template`, `test_each_conversation_gets_its_values_and_its_own_server_set`, `test_a_spec_parsed_outside_a_conversation_takes_the_defaults` | done |
| CV-D3 | **Fleets are keyed by (workspace root, digest of the conversation's values).** `register_server_set` appends `~cv-<digest>` to the server-set key when the conversation has values of its own (`plugins.servers.VARIANT_MARK`; the variant remembers its overlay, `variant_overlay`), and `tools/workspace_fleet.py` (`_active_tool_executor`'s body, moved out of `agent.py` at its cap) files the executor under `tools/fleet_key.fleet_key(root, variant)` = `root` or `root|cv=<digest>`, stamps `_clio_connector_values`, and starts stdio servers in the root. Conversations with no values share the plain fleet as before; two with the same values share theirs. The namespace stays the connector id (tool names, grants, `connector_setup`'s `partition('_')`, listing caches unchanged). Turn leases and residency holds stay keyed by the root and protect every fleet of the workspace (reaper lease lookups use `fleet_root`); `request_fleet_restart(root)` restarts every fleet of that root (`restart_every_fleet_at`); the view hold's fresh idle window notes every fleet of the root (`note_resolved_root`); `mcp_app_fleet` stamps a record with the session's own fleet; `connector_rebind` installs with the root as cwd. Net channels are filed by (root, namespace), so a reaped fleet closes them only when it was the last fleet of its root (typed `workspace_fleet_channels_kept`) | `tools/fleet_key.py`, `tools/workspace_fleet.py`, `tools/reaper.py`, `agent.py` (797 → 645 lines), `gact/view_fleet.py`, `gact/mcp_app_fleet.py`, `gact/connector_rebind.py` | `test_conversations_with_their_own_values_get_their_own_fleet`, `test_a_turn_lease_on_the_workspace_protects_every_fleet_there`, `test_fleet_keys`; existing fleet suites (`test_workspace_tool_executor`, `test_fleet_restart`, `test_workspace_fleet_reaper`, `test_view_fleet`, `test_mcp_app_fleet`, grants and storage boundary) pass; CV-Q1 to CV-Q4 live | passed (live) |
| CV-D4 | **`set_connector_value` writes this conversation's overlay** (refusals unchanged; no conversation → `connector_no_conversation`) and moves only this conversation: `connector_values.set_session_values` resolves the conversation's new fleet, lists the connector there (its state; unreachable gives the reason) and, inside a running turn, `LiveToolRefresher.move(namespace, fleet)` makes the turn's next step take that connector's tools from the new fleet while every other namespace stays on the fleet the turn started with. No other fleet is touched. Result adds `scope: "conversation"`; the row shows "Applies to: This conversation" | `gact/connector_tool.py`, `gact/connector_values.py`, `gact/agents/live_tool_refresh.py` | `test_several_values_are_set_with_one_apply` (updated), `test_a_conversation_value_is_not_a_default_and_save_as_default_is`, `test_a_running_turn_takes_a_moved_namespace_from_the_new_fleet`; CV-Q1, CV-Q2 live | passed (live) |
| CV-D5 | **A card in a conversation writes the overlay, with "Save as default" per value.** The setup card (CN5) and the offer card (CD-D2) read `GET /v1/connectors/{id}?session_id=` (value in use, `default_value`, `conversation`), show "This conversation only. Default: …" or "New conversations use it too." under each `address`/`value` row, and answer with `metadata.save_as_default: [keys]` (an unchanged ticked row sends its current value). A ticked value becomes the default and the conversation's own value for it is cleared (it follows the default); unticked it is the conversation's. A default change rebinds only the fleets whose connection changes (`connector_rebind_unchanged` for a fleet whose overlay covers it), so saving a default from one conversation never restarts another's server. A call waiting on a setup card runs on the fleet its turn started with, so when the card gave the conversation values of its own the call returns the typed `connector_values_set_for_conversation` and its turn's next call reaches the conversation's server. When an offer is accepted with values of the conversation's own, the connector joins the conversation's own fleet (`join_running_turn`). Extensions keeps editing defaults (`PUT /v1/connectors/{id}/values`) | `gact/connector_setup.py`, `gact/connector_offer.py`, `gact/routes/connectors.py`; gact-tui `connector-need-rows.tsx`, `connector-setup-response.tsx`, `connector-offer-response.tsx`, `use-connectors.ts`, `connector-draft.ts`, core `connector-domain.ts`, `connector-repository.ts` | `test_a_default_change_restarts_only_the_fleets_that_follow_it`, `test_turn_on_enables…` (offer values stay in the conversation), vitest `keeps a value to this conversation unless "Save as default" is ticked (CV-D5)`; CV-Q5 live | passed (live) |
| CV-D6 | **Reopen replays the saved call with the conversation's values in use at the time of the reopen** (the reopen runs under `_tool_session_context(sid)`, so it resolves the conversation's own fleet). If the values changed since, the server's reopen declaration (scene id) is replayed against the new place: ParaView answers the typed `unknown_scene` naming the scenes it has there, the card shows it with Retry, and no other conversation is affected | (no new code) | CV-Q6 live | passed (live) |
| CV-D7 | **Known limit, recorded:** a conversation whose own value equals the default still gets its own fleet (the digest is over the overlay, not over the difference from the defaults), because a later default change would otherwise move it to another server and stop its views. The agent clearing a value (`""`) removes it from the overlay | `connectors/overlay.py` | CV-Q6 (A's overlay became B's, `{prepare}`; the two conversations then share one fleet) | done |

| CV-N1 | With ParaView installed but not added to the conversation, the plain request "Open … in ParaView on this machine" was answered with `pvpython` from the shell and a PNG; `search_tools("paraview")` found nothing and the agent did not offer the connector (CV-Q2's first turn). "… with the ParaView connector" offered it every time | `conn-scope` / `discover`: the session facts or `search_tools`' miss should name an installed connector that is not in this conversation | fixed (`conn-fix`: CF-D3, CF-D4, CF-D7; CF-Q4 10/10) |
| CV-N2 | **An idle front stays after its fleet is reaped.** A call made on the shared plain fleet (A's first `scene_state`, before its values were set) left a front process with empty values. The plain fleet was later reaped (`grant_restart`, from the offer's join spec change), but the process stayed: the application's retained external connection (`gact/external_task_connections.py`, key `(server, auth_scope, proxy)`) still held its pipes, and it is retired only when a connection with the **same** server and spec supersedes it, which never comes once every conversation has its own values. The same idle front is AQ39's "second idle front process of the same workspace fleet with no ssh", so it predates this stream; per-conversation values make it more likely | Retire retained connections whose proxy belonged to a fleet when that fleet is closed (reaper close hook), typed | fixed (`conn-fix`: CF-D2, CF-D9; CF-Q2) |
| CV-N3 | The offer's join installs the connector in the turn's fleet with a spec that differs from the one its server set later declares (`PLUGIN_ROOT`/`PLUGIN_DATA`), giving `workspace_fleet_namespace_spec_changed` → deferred rebuild and `spec_conflict … first_mounted_kept` on the next resolve | `conn-scope` / `discover` (`join_namespace`): build the joined spec from the session's server-set declaration | fixed (`conn-fix`: CF-D1; CF-Q1) |
| CV-N4 | Pre-existing at the base: `check_file_size.py` fails on `clio_react.py` (817/804) and `plugins/store.py` (846/800) — FX-F4, unchanged here; `check_brand_literals.mjs` (`connector-presentation.ts:60`) and `check_theme_colors.mjs` fail on files this stream did not touch | — | open (pre-existing) |
### Permissions follow the mode (PM)

The person's rule: in **ask** every call that acts outside CLIO asks, with *Allow once*, *This session* and *This workspace*, so a granted tool asks once per session or workspace; in **bypass** nothing asks; once **auto** is polished it will accept most of them. Stack N=26 (agent 8826, UI 5226) on `ws/perm` at the integration head `3e8f1c990`, freshly restarted after each fix, `CLIO_CLOUD_ISOLATE=1`, `CLIO_ARC_CTE_FILE_CAPACITY=128MB`, connector fixtures on, clio-schemas 0.7.0b2 and clio-kit editable. Step files `perm_mode.sh` / `perm_mode.mjs` (rounds `bypass`, `ask-a`, `ask-a2ui`, `ask-b`, `ask-c`, `phone`) and `perm_hero.mjs`. Requests are plain except the A2UI surface, whose request names the tool and its fields (mechanism verified with a tool-directed prompt; not evidence of agent judgement). Screenshots: `/home/user/wt/gallery/perm/pm-*.png`.

| ID | Decision or finding | Where | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PM-F1 | **Found:** in `ask` a native tool with no rule or default ran without asking (`native_gate`): every `app__*` view tool and `set_connector_value`, which changes a connector's values and restarts its server | `gact/runtime/tool_permission_layers.py` | fixed by PM-D1, PM-D2 |
| PM-F2 | **Found:** `native_gate` read `session.approval_mode` and ignored a script's accepted mode (`code_mode/grants.approval_mode`) and `approval_profile`, unlike the gate | same | fixed by PM-D2, PM-D3 |
| PM-D1 | **The line is a declared property, not a name list:** `native_tool(..., outside=True)` (`OUTSIDE_ATTR`; `acts_outside(tool)` is true when declared and not `read_only`). A built-in declares it when it drives a server, a view the person sees or a connector's state *and* does not already ask through its own card or its own gate call. Declared now: every view tool (`mcp_app_tools._agent_tool`; `read_only` from the view's `readOnlyHint`) and `set_connector_value` (the declaration is the only change in `connector_tool.py`). Not declared: CLIO's bookkeeping (`search_tools`, `load_skill`, plans, todos, goals, memory, sessions, `look_at_view`), `offer_connector` (its card is the consent), and built-ins that already call the gate themselves (`ssh_execute`, `connected_data_connect`/`download`, `run_code`, NDP endpoint, plugin authoring, fleet). Plugin Python tools (`sdk.tool`) keep their declared `permission=` default and the person's rules (see PM-O1) | `gact/agents/tool_instrumentation.py`, `docs/PERMISSIONS.md` | done |
| PM-D2 | **An outside built-in passes the whole gate**, as an MCP tool does: `native_gate` calls `app.state.pending_permission_gate` (or `make_permission_gate()`) with `{kind: native, summary: <title>}`. So the session's mode, a script's accepted mode, `approval_profile`, SPOTTER clearance, PreToolUse hooks, plan locks, rules and defaults, `ai-review` and script grants decide it exactly as for MCP tools; `bypass` records `approval_mode_bypass`. Its answer stands for the tool's own nested checks (`_ANSWERED`). A refusal of any kind is recorded as a refused call (`notify_refused_call`, cancelled when the turn stopped) | `tool_permission_layers.py` | done |
| PM-D3 | Bookkeeping built-ins keep "run unless a rule or default says otherwise", now under the mode the gate reads (`script_grants.approval_mode`): a default `ask` is skipped under a script accepted in `bypass`; a rule deny is now also recorded as a refused call | same | done |
| PM-D4 | **The question names the tool by its title** (JR-F1 extended): the built-in's declared title (a view tool's name humanised, "Set highlight"; "Set connector value"), as MCP tools already read ("Open Board", "Ping", "Set Time"); an untitled built-in keeps "Use <name>" | `tool_permission_layers.py`, `permission_delivery.permission_summary` | done |
| PM-D5 | Grants stay keyed by the exact tool name (`_append_permission_policy_from_resolution`): `app__board__set_highlight`, `set_connector_value`, `board_open_board` | unchanged | done |
| PM-F3 | **Found live:** the agent calls the *recorded* wrapper of a built-in (`tool_recording.recording_tool` → `rebuilt_tool`), which copied the presentation markers but not `OUTSIDE_ATTR`, so the first bypass run (`sess_26148f97c5d8`) had no gate row for `set_connector_value`. `rebuilt_tool` now copies it; a test covers the recorded wrapper | `tool_instrumentation.rebuilt_tool` | fixed |
| PM-F4 | **Found live:** a surface's `PUT …/shown {shown:false}` for a view a newer view of its server had already replaced came back 404 (two failed requests per run). Unloading a closed view now answers `{shown:false}`; showing one still fails | `gact/mcp_app_look_routes.py` | fixed |
| PM-O1 | A plugin's Python tool (`clio_agent.sdk.tool`) that acts outside still runs unasked in `ask` unless its plugin declares `permission="ask"`. Giving `sdk.tool` an `outside=` flag is the same one-line declaration; left for the SDK owner, as it changes the plugin contract | `sdk/tools.py` | open |

Tests: `tests/test_gact/test_native_permission_mode.py` (ask asks by title; This session stops asking and a new session asks; This workspace carries; bypass records `approval_mode_bypass`; a deny rule holds in bypass; read-only view tools and bookkeeping do not ask; a script keeps its mode; a bookkeeping default reads the script's mode; the recorded wrapper keeps the declaration) and `test_mcp_app_look.py::test_unloading_a_view_the_server_closed_is_not_an_error`; with `test_tool_settings`, `test_permission_titles`, `test_code_mode_permissions`, `test_permission_gate`, `test_native_shell_approval`, `test_mcp_app_context_tools`, `test_tool_instrumentation`, connectors service/offer: 183 + 105 passed.

### Connectors per conversation (CS, stack N=24)

The person's decision: "one conversation shouldn't be burdened with the tokens of ParaView unless they decide they want it". Enabling a connector globally means installed and ready, nothing more; each conversation adds the connector's plugin to its agent. Stream `conn-scope`; plan CN9, CN10 and CD-D1 to CD-D4 *Amended*; contracts "Connectors" *Amended*.

| ID | Decision | Source | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CS-D1 | **Enable = installed and ready, nothing more.** The `scope` concept is gone: `Choice.scope`, `normalize_scope`, `applies_to`, `follow.applicable`/`additions`, the `agent_connectors` stamp (`CONNECTOR_FOLLOW_KEY`, `connector_stamp`), the `enable(scope)` argument and the offer's `_scope`. `POST …/enable` reads no body; a `scope` an older CLIO wrote in `connectors.yaml` is not read and is dropped on the next write. The connector row gains `plugin_ref`. UI: the card's "Where it applies" switch and agent checklist become "In conversations" (Enable / Turn off, and that a conversation adds it from its plugins); `scopeWords` and `connectorScopeSchema` are removed; "Add by URL or command" sends no scope | person's decision; plan CN10 *Amended* | `test_follow.py::test_enabling_keeps_no_scope_and_drops_an_older_one`, `test_conversation_plugins.py::test_enabling_adds_nothing_to_the_agent_and_lists_the_carrier` (same digest before and after enabling); CS-Q1 | passed (live) |
| CS-D2 | **The connector's plugin is in the catalog for every enabled connector.** `PluginService.catalog()` always adds `follow.carriers()`: a carrier `clio-connectors/connector-<id>` for every enabled connector (trusted, as extras were), so a delta naming one resolves in the resolver and in `resolve_subagent`. `GET /v1/plugins` (owner `gact/connector_plugins.py`) lists, besides installed plugins, the carriers of enabled connectors that no installed plugin follows (`follow.listed_carriers`); `/v1/plugins/inspect` finds them too. This fixes the gotcha that carriers existed only as resolver `extra` | stream brief | `test_follow.py::test_every_enabled_connector_has_a_carrier_*`; live `GET /v1/plugins`: `clio/paraview` and `clio-connectors/connector-burette`, both with `connector` | passed (live) |
| CS-D3 | **A connector turned off drops out with a typed reason, never a failure.** `follow.drop_off` removes from the combined delta (user agent's add, then the session's) every plugin whose connector is off, appending `removed` rows `{id, reason: "connector_off" \| "connector_not_found", because: <connector>}` and re-digesting. The session's delta keeps it, so enabling brings it back. `session_resolution` re-pins (note mode, `connector_follow.follow_connectors`) when `follow.drifted(pin)`: a plugin of a connector now off is in it, or a dropped one can come back. The sweep after a connector change (`follow_in_open_sessions`) re-pins only such conversations; `connector.changed.sessions` names them | stream brief; repo rule "no silent fallback" | `test_follow.py::test_a_delta_drops_*`, `::test_drifted_*`; `test_conversation_plugins.py::test_a_delta_adds_the_plugin_and_an_off_connector_drops_it_with_a_reason`, `::test_adding_to_one_conversation_leaves_the_other_and_follows_off_and_on`; CS-Q4 | passed (live) |
| CS-D4 | **An off connector is left out of the pickers** (not shown with an inline "Turn on"). Every plugin row that follows a connector carries `connector: {id, name, logo_url, enabled, status, needs, reason, tool_count}`; `offeredPlugins` keeps a connector plugin only while `connector.enabled`. The conversation's "Add plugin" picker (`agent-plugin-menu.tsx`, used by `agent-packages.tsx` and `agent-tab-drill-in.tsx`) lists them first under **Connectors**, titled by the connector's name with its logo, source "Connector" and its state in words (`pluginConnectorStatus`; "In this conversation" once added). The agent editor (`agent-composition-view.tsx` → save as agent) takes the same list, which is the existing per-agent default path. Kept out: the Extensions plugin rows (the connector row stands for it) and "Compose a plugin" (`extension-add-dialog.tsx`: a plugin bundle does not take a connector's plugin). ### Cross-app matrix (stack N=23, merged code)

The generic MCP App features on apps other than ParaView. Stack N=23 (agent 8823, UI 5223) on `ws/xapp` after merging the integration branch in clio-agent, gact-tui, clio-kit and the marketplace, `CLIO_CLOUD_ISOLATE=1`, `CLIO_ARC_CTE_FILE_CAPACITY=128MB`. Burette and SPICE enabled from the catalog; the fixtures saved as in "Stack fixtures" (`hostpanel`, `board`, `timeline`, `livecounter`). Step files `scripts/cloud/checks/apps/xapp_matrix.mjs` (one generic run per app: `XM_OPEN` plain request, then `card`, `tab`, `max`, `return`, `click`, `ctx`, `ask`, `look`, `comment`, `theme`, `close`, `restart`, `reopen`) and `xapp_dashboard.mjs` (a plain dashboard request, a plain follow-up while it is open, the captures the agent took). Every request is plain unless the cell says otherwise. Screenshots are in `/home/user/wt/gallery/xapp/` (names `xapp-<app>-f<feature>-…`), hero shots with the navigation collapsed and the canvas maximized; every one named here was read. A worker restart in the middle of the run took the first stack down; the rows after it are on a fresh start of the same configuration.

Apps: **A** Burette (hosted, Mol*/WebGL, `preview_pdb_structure`), **B** SPICE (`spice_plot_waveform`, `tests/fixtures/apps/rc_lowpass.cir`), **C1** host panel, **C2** board (the only app with view-registered app tools), **C3** timeline (channel), **C4** live counter (the service fixture), **D** ParaView (reference only, from earlier rows).

Features: **1** inline card, Open in tab, Return; **2** maximize and restore keep the view (a marker set in the view's window survives, no view frame navigates); **3** the view's reported context reaches the agent; **4** `look_at_view` describes the actual pixels; **5** a region comment answered from crop and context; **6** the app inside `clio.mcp-app.v1` in a dashboard, and the agent's own `capture_a2ui_surface` sees its render; **7** reopen after close and after a restart; **8** CLIO's theme; **9** app tools.

### Friction polish (FP, stack N=27)

The first-run journey's frictions (JR-P5 to JR-P15) and the integrator's watch items, fixed on `ws/polish` and checked on stack N=27 (agent 8827, UI 5227), first with the neutral brand, then (after merging `f84acd0d3`) under the CLIO brand. A fresh `.cloud-run/xdg`; data in `.cloud-run/sci-data/` (`wavelet.vti`, `pulse_series.pvd` with 8 steps, `1crn.pdb`), outside any workspace. Step files: `fp_settings.mjs`, `fp_frame.mjs`, `fp_dash.mjs`, `fp_dash_capture.mjs`, `fp_comment.mjs`, and `journey.mjs`/`journey_enable.mjs`. Screenshots (all read) are in `/home/user/wt/gallery/polish/` (`fp<item>-…`). Console errors and failed requests were empty in every run except Burette's own upstream lines (`403` on its assets, `xr-spatial-tracking`, `styleToolbar` at phone width) and one `409` from `a2ui/visual-feedback` while the dashboard was being built.

#### Decisions

| ID | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| FP-D1 | `pnpm lint` and `pnpm typecheck` are clean on the branch: canvas colours read theme tokens (`readThemeColor`: `media-backdrop`/`media-foreground` for a capture's gap note, `muted`/`border`/`foreground` for a refused view, `primary`/`primary-foreground` for comment overviews); the connector "Starting…" detail takes the integration branch's wording. |
| FP-D2 | **A view is named by connector and subject**, `"<connector>: <subject>"`, server side (`gact/mcp_app_label.py`), for the live part, a reopened part and the saved call a surface shows. Connector: the saved connection's label, else the catalog entry, else the tool title, else the namespace. Subject: the opening call's first file- or URL-like argument in declared order (last path segment), else its first single-line string of at most 80 characters. The colon, not a middle dot (the product forbids U+00B7). A call that names only a scene id is labelled by it ("ParaView: pulse-series-122aee23"); the view's own reported title was not used, since no host message carries one. Contract in `mcp-apps-contracts.md`, "A view's label". |
| FP-D3 | **A first run's conversations get their own folder**: a fresh `ws_default` is seeded with `<data>/workspace` unless `paths.default_workspace` is set (an existing one keeps its root); a probed stdio server (enabling a connector) starts there too. A turn's stdio servers already start in the session's workspace (`_build_tool_gateway(cwd=root)`). The fence creates its empty mount points (`.git`, `.aws`, `.codex`, `.agents`) in that folder, as in any workspace. |
| FP-D4 | **Connectors are findable**: the nav item reads "Extensions and connectors" (search keywords add connectors, apps, views, services); a bare connection label as the Agent heading reads "Agent: cloud"; under the Connectors filter, Yours suggests nothing unrelated and Discover lists the catalog's connectors that are off ("Connector catalog") before "MCP servers you add yourself" and the MCP Registry (moved to `extension-registry-section.tsx`, no behaviour change). |
| FP-D5 | **Names, not ids, in notices**: "Plugins changed: +Burette (4 new tools)" names a following plugin, a connector's plugin and a carrier after the connector, and other ids read as words; a reopen error's trailing `[code]` moves to a "Technical details" line; the connector card says "Agent guidance: How to work with ParaView" instead of "Brings plugin paraview". |
| FP-D6 | **Comments**: the region editor is the mark's badge and the view's label, the comment (focused), then Delete/Cancel/Done; the region's numbers are in a collapsed "Adjust region" (keyboard users); no separator character in the header (layout gap). It opens clear of the "Add N marks" tray (left of it, else above, else below) and re-places itself when it grows. Inside a dashboard a chart's own capture reads "Mark regions in this chart" and its controls offer "Mark across the whole dashboard"; the frame menu row reads "Mark regions across the dashboard". |
| FP-D7 | **Who made a change**: every call a view makes (`_run_bound`: a view's `tools/call`, a surface's channel call and its opening, a reopen) carries `_meta["ai.iowarp.clio/origin"] = "view"` (`tools/call_origin.py`, added by `TaskAwareClient.call_tool_mcp`); ParaView's front reads it and its node half records the change as the person's. A surface's own opening of its view is therefore "person: opened": the person opened the dashboard. Contract "Call origin". |
| FP-D8 | ParaView `_show` clears a colour only when one is set: the PDB reader's representation has no association (`None`), and `ColorBy(rep, None)` raised `invalid association string 'NONE'`, so every `set_representation` on a PDB rolled back. |
| FP-D9 | **Truthful frame state and Reload view**: after `initialized` the host looks at the view with the snapshot at 0.25 scale (at 0.8, 2.5 and 6 s, and 0.8 s after a resize; never closing or expiring the view): "Drawing" until something shows, then "Ready"; "Nothing drawn" (with a notice and a Reload view button) when nothing is painted, when under 1.5% of the picture differs from its main colour, or under 3% with its WebGL drawing silent. Measured live: Burette blank at phone width 0.8 to 1.04%, drawn about 6%. The frame's More menu has "Reload view" (mounts the frame afresh; the server keeps its state). The header wraps at narrow widths. |
| FP-D10 | **One name on the first screen**: the welcome composer names the agent in its "with" chip ("Ask CLIO anything…"), the brand only until the agents load. |
| FP-D11 | **A view's state keys are data**: a view tool may declare `_meta["ai.iowarp.clio/state"]` (`[{key, tool?, description?}]`); with the keys a live view last reported, they appear in the view's context block ("The state it reports…: time (set with set_timestep), …"), in `a2ui_mcp_app_tool_not_found` per view tool, and `a2ui_mcp_app_state_key_unknown` refuses a channel key a declaring tool does not list. The block reads the declaration from the fleet that opened the view (the active executor at turn start is the default one). ParaView's `open_view` declares its keys. Contract "A view's state keys". |

### Connector follow-ups (CF)

Stream `conn-fix`, on `ws/conn-values` after merging the integration head (conn-scope and conn-values merged): the three findings `conn-values` handed over (CV-N1 to CV-N3), the cosmetic one `conn-scope` left open, and three requests of the person's that came in during the work: compact connector cards (CF-D5), an offer of any plugin, not only a connector's (CF-D2 to CF-D7), and an offer list built on the marketplaces the person adds (CF-D4). Plan CD-D1 and CD-D2 *Amended*; contracts "Connectors" *Amended*; site `connectors.mdx` and `agents-and-plugins.mdx` *Amended*.

#### Decisions

| ID | Decision | Where | Evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CF-D1 | **A connector that joins a running turn takes the spec its conversation's server set declares** (CV-N3). `join_running_turn` asks `connector_rebind.conversation_spec(app, session, namespace, root)`: the session's server set (`session_server_set`, which now includes the plugin the offer just added) built the way the fleet builds it (`load_agent_mcp_servers(cwd=root, plugin_servers=server_set_declarations(key))`), so the joined spec has the plugin's `PLUGIN_ROOT` and `PLUGIN_DATA`, the conversation's values and source `plugin:<id>`, and the fleet's next resolve finds it equal (`server_set_spec_changed` false, the merge reports it `already`). When the set does not declare it, the typed trace `connector_join_spec_undeclared reason=not_in_server_set\|no_conversation\|<resolution reason>` and the join builds the spec from the saved connection as before; a plain plugin's server with no declared spec is not joined (`plugin_join_undeclared`) | `gact/connector_rebind.py`, `gact/connector_offer.py` | `test_connector_follow_ups.py::test_the_conversation_spec_is_the_one_its_server_set_declares`, `::test_a_joined_connector_is_not_a_changed_spec_on_the_next_resolve` (the old join reproduces `spec_changed`; the new one does not, and the merge reports `already`, no conflict), `::test_an_undeclared_namespace_is_typed_and_the_join_uses_the_connection`, `::test_join_running_turn_joins_with_the_conversation_spec`; CF-Q1 live | passed (live) |
| CF-D2 | **A fleet's dropped proxies retire the connections the application kept on them** (CV-N2). New owner `tools/fleet_close_listeners.py`: the reaper (`idle_ttl`, `lru_cap`, `grant_restart`), `request_restart` (`restart`), the workspace fleet's configuration eviction (`configuration_switched`) and a connector rebind of a namespace (`namespace_rebound`, the replaced proxy) call `proxies_dropped(root, proxies, reason)`; `gact/external_task_connections.watch_fleets` (registered by `ExternalTasks`, removed by `close_connections`) retires each retained connection keyed `in-process:<proxy>` on the application loop, typed `external_mcp_connection_fleet_closed server=… retired=… fleet=… because=…`. An idle one closes now; a busy one is marked superseded and now closes once its last call's custody ends (`retire_connection` treats a superseded shared connection like an isolated one), which also fixes the same leak for `_retire_superseded`. A listener that raises is typed `fleet_close_listener_failed` and never breaks a close | `tools/fleet_close_listeners.py`, `tools/reaper.py`, `tools/workspace_fleet.py`, `gact/connector_rebind.py`, `gact/external_task_connections.py`, `gact/external_tasks.py` | `test_connector_follow_ups.py::test_listeners_hear_dropped_proxies_and_a_failing_one_is_typed`, `::test_the_reaper_tells_listeners_which_fleet_it_closed`, `::test_a_closed_fleets_retained_connection_is_retired` (idle retired, busy superseded, another proxy's kept); CF-Q2 live | passed (live) |
| CF-D3 | **One offer tool for any plugin: `offer_plugin(plugin, reason)`** replaces `offer_connector`. `plugin` is a connector (id or name), a plugin that follows one (its connector's offer, unchanged: enable, needs, values), or any other plugin of the person's library (`id` or `marketplace/id`): the same `connector_setup` card with `intent: "add_plugin"` (`plugin_id`, `plugin_ref`, the reason; no needs). Add puts the plugin in the session delta as a `note`-mode change (`connector_follow.add_plugins`, shared with the connector path: the `plugin_change` injection announces it); its MCP servers join the running turn with the declared spec (CF-D1), its skills load in the same turn (CF-D6), its Python tools come with the next message (typed `plugin_tools_next_message`). Typed outcomes: `plugin_offer_declined` (refusal), `plugin_offer_cancelled`, `plugin_not_found`, `plugin_offer_no_conversation`; a conversation that cannot take it answers the card 422 with the resolution's code. A built-in plugin, a pack, an untrusted plugin with executable parts and one already here are not offered (already here: `already_here: true`, no card). **Listed, not deferred** (`native_tool("offer_plugin", defer=False)`): the session facts name what it offers, so a search for it is a wasted step (the first ParaView run of CF-Q4 loaded it with `select:offer_plugin`). Presentation: "Offer <name>" (`plugin_offer_presentation.py`); expansion shows the reason, the answer, Where: This conversation, the skills, the tools, and what comes with the next message; "List" with the rows when no plugin is named; the connector case keeps its presentation and adds its plugin's skills | `gact/plugin_offer.py`, `gact/plugin_offer_presentation.py`, `gact/connector_offer.py`, `gact/connector_setup.py`, `gact/connector_follow.py`, core `tools/runtime.py`, `native_tool_registry.py` | `test_plugin_offer.py` (15 tests: card payload, Add with servers and skills, Not now, 422, stop, connector routing, listing, unknown, presentation with 12 skills and 25 tools, Python tools typed), `test_offer.py` (connector case, `tool_name: offer_plugin`), `test_tool_instrumentation.py`; CF-Q3, CF-Q4 live | passed (live) |
| CF-D4 | **What the agent sees: one compact list in the session facts** (`session_facts_plugins`, replaces `session_facts_connectors`; config `plugins.offer_listed`, default 20, replaces `connectors.offer_listed`). Connectors first (`Name (id, connector, on\|off): summary`), then the plugins of **the marketplaces the person added** (`PluginStore.marketplaces()`, built-ins excluded, no marketplace hard-coded), grouped by marketplace with the most recently added first, each `name [marketplace]: summary` (the description's first 100 characters). So a marketplace the person adds is never hidden behind the cap by the default one; a removed marketplace's plugins are gone. The total is always given; past the cap, "offer_plugin with no plugin lists them all". The prose says what the list means (what they bring is not yours until the person adds one; on Add it is yours from the next step in the same turn) and nothing about when to offer. **Cost** (cl100k count, an approximation of the model's tokenizer): 658 tokens for the live 20-row list (39 offerable: 3 connectors, 35 of the default marketplace, 1 of trade-lab), against about 120 for the three-connector list before; `offer_plugin` listed adds about 150. **Direction (not done now):** the default marketplace holds every plugin for convenience; later the core marketplace stays and the person adds domain marketplaces (science, Factorio, OPAL, …), so each person's list shrinks to what they chose. The list is already built on that model | `gact/plugin_offer.py` (`library`, `offerable`), injection `session_facts_plugins.md`, `agents/composition.py`, `config.defaults.yaml` | `test_plugin_offer.py::test_the_list_has_connectors_then_library_plugins_not_here`, `::test_the_facts_are_capped_say_so_and_cost_little`, `::test_a_second_marketplace_is_offered_and_a_removed_one_is_not` (a second marketplace's plugins appear, first; removed, they do not; the default's do not when it is removed); live facts block in `sess_0d14a45b347f` | passed (live) |
| CF-D5 | **Compact cards** (the person: "too many line, line, line, details, line"). The offer card is three rows: the connector's logo (a puzzle icon for a plugin), "Add <name> to this conversation?" and the state in words at the right ("Enabled", "Not enabled (Add turns it on)", "Plugin from <marketplace>"); the agent's reason on one line, truncated, the whole of it on click (`aria-expanded`); Add, Not now and a quiet "Settings (n)" disclosure for the optional rows. Dropped: the description subtitle (it is in Extensions), "Why the agent asks", "Nothing is required to set up.", "Its tools join this conversation only." Required needs show only when there are some. "Adding turns … on" and a later sign-in are in Add's tooltip and the spoken detail. The setup card drops its separate logo/name/state row: the logo leads the header, the state is at its right, "<tool> waits for this, then continues." is one line. Numbered keys kept (1 Add, Enter, Esc). `AskCard` takes a `leading` node for the logo. The middle dot the brief proposed fails `check_middle_dot_copy.mjs`, so it reads "Not enabled (Add turns it on)" | gact-tui `connector-offer-response.tsx`, `connector-setup-response.tsx`, `connector-setup-activity.tsx`, `ask-card.tsx`, core `connector-domain.ts` (`isPluginOffer`, `plugin_id`, `plugin_ref`) | `connector-offer.test.tsx` (three rows, nothing dropped shows, reason expands, Settings in the action row, Enabled with no needs row, the plugin card and its line), `connector-setup.test.tsx`; CF-Q5 live | passed (live) |
| CF-D6 | **A skill the conversation gained mid-turn loads in that turn.** `load_skill` reads the turn's skills at call time and, for an id it lacks, `agents/skill_join.joined_skill` resolves it from the conversation's pinned resolution (the skill is in it: reality, not matching) and adds it to the turn's runtime, typed `skill_joined_turn`; anything else stays "unknown skill". The offer's result names the skills (`skills: [{id, description}]`, for a connector's plugin too) | `gact/agents/skill_join.py`, `gact/agents/skill_runtime.py`, `gact/connector_offer.py` | `test_plugin_offer.py::test_a_skill_the_conversation_gained_mid_turn_loads`, `::test_a_connector_offer_names_the_skills_its_plugin_brings`; CF-Q3 (`skill_joined_turn skill=tariff-landed-cost`) | passed (live) |

| CF-Q5 | The compact cards at 1440×900 and 390×844 | `connector_cards_compact.mjs` with the fixture connectors (mechanism verified with a near-plain request; the second message is a scripted follow-up). Offer card with a required row: title, "Not enabled (Add turns it on)" (phone: "Not enabled", the title keeps its two lines), the reason on one line, Access token, Add / Not now / Settings (1) (`cf-compact-offer-required-token-desktop.png`, `cf-compact-offer-settings-open-desktop.png`, `cf-compact-offer-required-token-phone.png`; the phone shot before the narrow fix `…-before-narrow-fix.png` cut the title to "Add Fixture Secret to…"). Setup card: logo, "Set up Fixture Secret", "Needs access token", one line "fixture-secret_fixture_secret_check waits for this, then continues.", the token row, Continue / Decline (`cf-compact-setup-card-desktop.png`, `cf-compact-setup-card-phone.png`); Continue returned the call's result (`cf-compact-setup-answered-desktop.png`). ParaView and plugin cards: CF-Q3, CF-Q4 shots | UI | passed (live) |

(`sess_d245473b9ce4`: `fs_write_file` only) and "Convert 72 degrees Fahrenheit to Celsius and explain the formula in one line." #### Findings

| ID | Finding | Owner / proposal | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CF-N1 | **CV-N1's diagnosis.** In CV-Q2 the agent saw (1) the facts line "ParaView (paraview, on): …" and "offer it with offer_connector (search_tools loads it)", (2) `offer_connector` only as a name among core's deferred tools, (3) `search_tools("paraview")` → "No deferred tool matches" with no word that a connector not in this conversation is not searched, and (4) a request that told it how to run the local ParaView ("ParaView here needs export PATH=… first"), with `pvpython` reachable from the shell. Nothing told it that adding the connector would give it the tools in the same turn. Grounded by CF-D4, CF-D3 and CF-D7; CF-Q4 10/10 | `conn-fix` | fixed (CF-D3, CF-D4, CF-D7) |
| CF-N2 | The transcript row "Plugins changed: +connector-fixture-secret (1 new tool)" names a connector's plugin by its id (the label is parsed from the `plugin_change` note, `pluginChangeLabel`); naming it by the connector needs the note or the row to carry the connector's name | gact-tui `plugin-presentation.ts` and the `plugin_change_added` injection | open (cosmetic) |
| CF-N3 | The agent usually runs `search_tools` before `offer_plugin` even with the tool listed and the plugin in the facts (one or two wasted steps, about 2 s each) | — | open (observed; no handcuff added) |
| CF-N4 | A connector offer card has no reason row when the agent leaves `reason` empty (CF-Q3's first run); the argument is optional | — | open (recorded) |
| CF-N5 | Left in the stream's isolated config (`.cloud-run/xdg`, gitignored): the `trade-lab` fixture marketplace, installed and trusted; ParaView enabled with its default prepare value | — | done (recorded) |
| CF-N6 | Pre-existing, unchanged: `check_file_size.py` (`clio_react.py`, `plugins/store.py`, CV-N4) and `check_theme_colors.mjs` (`a2ui-app-capture.ts`, `mcp-app-comment-images.ts`) | — | open (pre-existing) |

### CI gates (GT)

Stream `gates`, on `ws/polish` after merging the integration head (clio-agent `a1a1ef285`, gact-tui `8718733b4`): every gate the four repositories' CI runs, on the merged code, with no live stack. "Base" is the merge base with `origin/develop` (`54ae350af` in clio-agent) or `origin/main` (clio-kit `c22c7e4`, clio-schemas `fd04806`). The unit suite ran at `-n 2` on a shared 4-CPU machine; a failure that passed when its file was rerun on its own is recorded as load, not as a fault.
